    <!-- ai-memory-managed: routing-skill -->
    
    # ai-memory durable pages
    
    Use this skill only for deliberate durable wiki mutations. Routine session capture is automatic, and permanent notes require an explicit user request.
    
    ## Tools in this cluster
    
    - `memory_resolve_write_target` validates and canonicalizes an exact existing
      writable destination without creating or changing it. Bulk importers use it
      before their first write.
    - `memory_write_page` writes or replaces a durable wiki page for permanent project knowledge.
    - `memory_write_pages_batch` writes a bounded set of complete page versions
      atomically for a bulk importer. Every entry must be conditional; do not use
      it for routine notes.
    - `memory_patch_page` atomically changes named Markdown sections in an existing
      page without re-emitting its frontmatter or unrelated body.
    - `memory_delete_page` removes a durable wiki page by exact path.
    - `memory_restore_page` lists a page's checkpoints (no `rev`) or writes an
      earlier version back (with `rev`), including a deleted page. It is admin
      only on a multi-user server and does not reach `scope: "profile"` pages.
    
    ## Writing durable memory
    
    Write a page only when the user explicitly asks to remember something permanently, save a note, add an annotation, or record project knowledge. Do not use durable pages for transient progress, normal status updates, or next-session context.
    
    Put the page title as a `# H1` on the first line of the body and omit the separate title argument. ai-memory derives the title from that H1. Keep the content concise and fact-like, with enough context that a future agent can apply it without rereading the whole session.
    
    Optional top-level metadata: `kind` (64 characters), `entities` (10 names,
    64 characters each, normalized and deduplicated), `abstract` (1,024 characters),
    and `relations` (an object mapping `causes`, `fixes`, or `contradicts` to page
    target arrays, 32 targets total, 1,024 characters each). Relation targets use
    `path`, `project:path`, or `workspace/project:path`; extensionless page paths
    resolve with `.md`, and scope components must already be trimmed. A page that
    describes repository files can name them in `source_files`: up to 16
    `{path, commit}` entries, each a repository-relative `/`-separated path and the
    7-64 hex commit (`git rev-parse HEAD`) the page was checked against. The server
    never reads those files; when one changed since its commit, a client records
    `memory_feedback` with `signal: "stale"`, and rewriting the page with the new
    commit clears the flag. Limits apply
    to raw values before trimming, normalization or deduplication. Arbitrary
    frontmatter and author fields are not editable.
    Every `memory_write_page` call replaces the whole page and its metadata;
    omitted fields are cleared. Read the current page first when preserving
    existing content matters, and pass its returned `page_id` as
    `expected_page_id` so a concurrent change fails without writing. Use
    `create_only: true` when the page must not already exist; do not combine the
    two preconditions.
    
    For a targeted edit, prefer `memory_patch_page`: read the page, pass its
    required `page_id` as `expected_page_id`, and submit one bounded batch.
    `append` with `target: "section"` takes a heading `anchor` from the read
    response, and with `target: "end_of_page"` takes none; `add_section` takes a
    new level 2-6 heading such as `## Decisions`; `replace_section` takes an
    `anchor` and must also carry that section's returned `sha256` as
    `section_sha256`.
    All edits resolve against the same captured body and either commit together or
    write nothing. Missing, duplicate, stale, or conflicting anchors fail closed.
    Use `memory_write_page` when replacing the whole document.
    
    When the user explicitly wants a note to be temporary, pass `expires_at` as an
    RFC3339 instant or a bare `YYYY-MM-DD` (the end of that day in UTC). After the
    TTL, normal search, recent, and briefing reads hide the page; the next forget
    sweep deletes it. A TTL outranks `pinned`, so do not combine them unless the user
    has deliberately requested that behavior.
    
    ## Writing a session's pages yourself
    
    When the user asks to consolidate the session you are taking part in, write its pages with your own model instead of the server's: read the session's raw observations first (the retrieval skill covers that read), then write each page with `memory_write_page` passing the same `session_id`, which records the session as evidence and settles its queued consolidation job. Match the server's multi-page layout, at most five pages:
    
    - `sessions/<session_id>.md`: the session narrative, `tier: "episodic"`, `kind: "fact"`.
    - `concepts/<slug>.md`: evergreen concept pages, `tier: "semantic"`, `kind: "fact"`.
    - `decisions/<short>.md`: ADR-style records of a choice made, `tier: "semantic"`, `kind: "decision"`.
    - `gotchas/<slug>.md`: failure modes and surprises, `tier: "semantic"`, `kind: "gotcha"`.
    - `_rules/<slug>.md`: a durable project convention written as a standalone instruction, `tier: "semantic"`, `kind: "rule"`.
    
    Only split out what the session actually established; a short session is one session page. Record what was said and done, and never turn a user's question into a confirmed decision.
    
    ## Project rules belong in instructions first
    
    If the user asks to create a durable project rule such as always do X or never do Y, update the project's canonical agent instruction file when the repository says one exists. Use a durable page only when the user explicitly wants the rule in the wiki too, or when no canonical instruction file applies.
    
    ## Deleting durable memory
    
    Delete only by exact path. If the user gives a vague title or topic, first resolve it to the page path using read-only lookup. Preserve sibling projects unless the user explicitly names them.
    
    ## Project scope
    
    Choose scope from the MCP client's identity support:
    
    - **Session-aware MCP clients** that forward the real lifecycle-hook session id on every request should use automatic current-project routing. Omit `workspace`, `project`, and `cwd` for the current repository; pass explicit scope only when the user names a different project.
    - **Static MCP clients** (including clients with lifecycle hooks but no bridge connecting that hook session id to MCP requests) must pass `workspace` and `project` together on every project-scoped call, including requests about this project, here, or our work. Read the exact names from the nearest `.ai-memory.toml` when it declares both. Without a marker override, derive the project from normalized `upstream`, then `origin`, using the full repository path without its host (`github.com/acme/api` → `acme-api`); use the folder basename only when no valid remote exists. Never rely on the server's last active project.
    
    This rule applies only to project-scoped calls. For cross-project retrieval, `global=true` must omit `workspace`, `project`, and `scopes`. For a standing preference written with `scope: "global"`, omit `workspace` and `project`.
    
    ## Architectural decisions get ADR structure and a pin
    
    When the user asks to record an architectural decision (a chosen approach, a rejected alternative, a standing trade-off), write it as a durable page under `decisions/<short-slug>.md` with `pinned: true` and this structure in the body:
    
    ```markdown
    # <Decision title>
    
    **Status:** accepted   <!-- proposed | accepted | superseded by [[decisions/other]] -->
    
    ## Context
    What situation forced a decision; the constraints that mattered.
    
    ## Decision
    What was decided, stated as a fact.
    
    ## Consequences
    What becomes easier, what becomes harder, what was given up.
    Rejected alternatives and WHY, so future sessions don't re-propose them.
    ```
    
    Pinned pages are exempt from retention decay and curation, and the auto-improvement path refuses to rewrite them — the record stays immutable unless a human unpins it. To supersede a decision, write a NEW page and set the old page's status line to `superseded by [[decisions/<new>]]`; never edit the old decision's substance. Note: ai-memory never touches files in the project repository — a `docs/adr/` directory managed there (by hand or by another tool) is outside ai-memory entirely; these wiki ADRs complement it for cross-session retrieval.
    
    ## Standing user/team preferences go to the cross-project profile
    
    When the fact is a standing preference that should apply to EVERY project — technology choices ("always use pnpm workspaces"), code style ("prefer composition over inheritance"), personal conventions ("never `--force` without asking") — call `memory_write_page` with `scope: "profile"` instead of the current project. The page joins the cross-project profile under `profile/` (pass a path like `tools/pnpm.md`; it is stored as `profile/tools/pnpm.md`), every project's session start lists it as a default, and default memory reads surface it as `global_scope_hits`. Start the body with a `# Title` and one imperative line; pass `applies_to` (e.g. `["rust"]`) to scope it to projects on that stack, and `enforced_by` (e.g. `"pre-push hook"`) when something already enforces it, which keeps it out of the session-start digest; both are accepted only for profile entries, and a rewrite that omits them clears them. Remove one with `memory_delete_page` and `scope: "profile"`. `scope: "global"` still writes the shared `_global` scope directly. Neither scope can be combined with workspace/project arguments. Use it only for genuinely cross-project preferences; project-specific rules stay in the project (or its instruction file, per the section above). A rules file always wins over the profile: the profile is the default for what the repository does not say.

## Activity

    $ ai-memory --help
    `ai-memory` binary entry point.
    
    Usage: ai-memory [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>
    
    Commands:
      init                        Initialise the data directory layout
      setup                       Set up a native install in one step: `init`, a user service (systemd `--user` or a launchd LaunchAgent), and hooks, MCP and skills for every detected agent, then a read-only self-check. Shows the plan and asks first unless `--yes` is given. Re-running changes nothing
      status                      Print runtime status (counts, paths, version)
      list-projects               List every workspace/project pair the server knows about (plain, scriptable output — the read-only equivalent of hitting `GET /api/v1/projects` directly, falling back to `GET /admin/projects` when the server runs without `--enable-web` or `--enable-api`)
      doctor                      Check capture coverage: compare local harness session stores for this project against what the server captured, and warn when a harness ran here recently but has no captured sessions (its hook is likely missing). Also reports agentmemory MCP entries and rules-file guideline blocks left after a migration; it only reads and prints the fix
      backfill                    One-time import of this project's existing local harness session history into a brand-new (empty) ai-memory store, so installing hooks mid-project doesn't start amnesiac. No-op once the store has any sessions unless `--force`
      repair-backfill-timestamps  Correct `sessions.started_at`/`ended_at` for sessions that `backfill` already imported before it carried the transcript's own event times, by re-reading the local transcripts and matching them by session id. Dry-run by default; `--confirm` applies
      run                         Launch an agent in an opt-in, cross-harness managed workstream. Native arguments are forwarded except exact wrapper flags such as `--yolo`, `--fresh`, and `--force-unlock`
      show                        Pick a local project and installed harness, then launch from that checkout. Removes the `cd` step `run` requires
      continue                    Resume the most recently launched managed checkout from anywhere, without `cd` and without picking from a list
      resume                      Interactively pick a managed workstream in the current checkout and launch harness
      handoffs                    List open cross-agent handoffs so a stale one can be cancelled by id
      message                     Send, list, pop, or cancel cross-project agent messages (a directed, claim-once mailbox between two projects — see `docs/agent-messaging.md`)
      workstreams                 List recent managed workstreams selectable from the current checkout
      rename-workstream           Rename a managed workstream in the current checkout. Metadata only: the ledger, linked harnesses, and which workstream a bare `ai-memory run` resumes are all unchanged
      workstream-search           Search the complete visible event ledger for a managed workstream
      audit-contamination         Audit the store for likely cross-project contamination (read-only, SQL-only). Flags sessions whose cwd resolves to a different project and observations whose project disagrees with their session
      search                      Full-text search the wiki via FTS5
      recent                      List the most recently updated wiki pages
      read-page                   Fetch and display the full body of a wiki page. Accepts either `--path` (exact path) or a positional query that searches FTS5 and fetches the top matching page
      write-page                  Write or update a wiki page atomically (also indexes it in the store)
      delete-page                 Delete a single wiki page. The server routes scope resolution through `resolve_ws_proj`, so a delete targeting a project that exists in multiple workspaces never silently lands in the wrong slot (the MCP `memory_delete_page` had that gap until this build)
      serve                       Run the MCP server (with watcher) over stdio or HTTP
      reset                       Wipe the data directory's wiki/, db/, raw/ contents
      compact                     Reclaim free database pages. Deletes nothing
      reclaim-ledger-versions     Drop the superseded ledger versions the pre-2.1.1 indexer left behind
      backup                      Snapshot wiki/, db/, and an in-data-dir config.toml into a gzipped tarball
      backup-agents               Snapshot AI agent configurations, skills, and plugins into a portable archive
      export-okf                  Export one project's wiki as an OKF v0.2 bundle tarball
      restore                     Restore a backup tarball into the data directory
      restore-agents              Restore AI agent configurations, skills, and plugins from an archive
      reindex                     Rebuild the SQLite index from the wiki/ markdown (the "DB is rebuildable from files" guarantee). Recreates workspaces/projects from each scope's `_meta.md` manifest and reindexes every page. Run with the server stopped, against a freshly-migrated (clean) data dir
      install-hooks               Print (or apply) lifecycle-hook configuration for an agent CLI
      hook                        Emit a single lifecycle hook natively (reads the event payload from stdin), avoiding a shell spawn. Used by the WindowsNative hook config; mirrors hooks/<agent>/<event>.sh
      install-mcp                 Print MCP server registration snippets for any supported client. Current values are listed under `--client`; see docs/mcp-install.md for the full guide
      commit                      Stage + commit the wiki tree under git
      checkpoints                 List recent wiki git checkpoints for recovery
      restore-page                Restore a single wiki page from a git checkpoint and reindex it
      llm-test                    Smoke-test an LLM provider by sending one prompt
      forget-sweep                Run the M8 retention sweep over episodic pages
      lint                        Run the M8 lint pass (stale / duplicates + optional LLM contradiction)
      curator                     Run the rule-based curator report
      auto-improve-report         Run a read-only auto-improvement telemetry report
      auto-improve                Run auto-improvement for one completed session
      finalize-session            Manually finalize the latest open session for one agent in this project
      recover                     Recover history a server outage missed: drain the local hook spool, replay degraded-run transcripts through the hook backfill path, and finish managed runs whose transcript import failed. Safe to re-run
      pending-writes              Review, approve, or reject staged auto-improvement proposals
      embed                       Compute + store embeddings for every latest page (M9)
      generate-auth-token         Generate a random hex bearer token for AI_MEMORY_AUTH_TOKEN
      setup-agent                 One-shot agent setup for docker deploys: extract the bundled hook scripts to a host-mounted directory AND print the matching config snippet. Replaces the "clone the repo + cargo build" workflow for users who never want a local Rust toolchain
      bootstrap                   Pre-load an existing project's history into the wiki by LLM-summarising git log, README, docs/, and module headers into seed wiki pages. Run once when adopting ai-memory in a project that's been around for a while. Requires AI_MEMORY_LLM_PROVIDER configured on the server
      install-instructions        Install the ai-memory usage snippet and managed Agent Skills into the project (or any markdown file / skill root you specify). Idempotent — bracketed by `<!-- ai-memory:start -->` / `<!-- ai-memory:end -->` markers so re-running replaces the block in place without duplicating
      install-skills              Install core-managed ai-memory Agent Skills into agent skill directories
      reorg                       Retro-fit existing sessions + observations to per-cwd projects based on the cwd captured at session-start. Pages are marked `is_latest=false` (they were a multi-project mash-up) so the next consolidation can regenerate them per-project. Idempotent
      purge-project               Delete a project: its pages, sessions, observations, handoffs, embeddings, managed workstreams and on-disk wiki files
      purge-session               Permanently delete ONE session and everything derived from it
      rename-project              Rename a project within its workspace. No files move on disk — the wiki is flat and pages are differentiated by project_id only. Useful after renaming the project's directory on disk so the hook router keeps writing into the same logical project
      repair-split                Join a checkout split in two by its git remote's arrival: move the empty remote-named project's repository identity onto the project that holds the sessions, and keep the empty one under an archive name. Nothing is deleted
      move-project                Move a project into another workspace. A fresh destination is a lossless TRUE MOVE (re-stamp workspace_id, keep project_id, rename the dir) — sessions/observations/handoffs and history all survive. A destination that already holds a same-named project MERGES via copy+purge (only durable pages migrate, source purged). Either way the operation is irreversible — requires `--confirm`
      merge-project               Losslessly merge one project into another inside the same workspace. Without `--confirm`, prints an exact server-side dry run
      move-session                Move one session (or every session of a project) to another project, re-stamping its observations, handoffs, consolidation jobs and its `sessions/<id>.md` page in one transaction. Without `--confirm` it prints what would move (a real dry run, rolled back server-side)
      uninstall                   Remove ai-memory's wiring (hooks, MCP, instructions, and default-root managed skills) from all detected agents. Dry-run unless `--apply`
      upgrade                     Upgrade a GitHub-release native install: download the matching release asset, verify its `.sha256`, atomically replace this binary (and sibling `hooks/` when present), then re-stage hooks for agents already under the data-dir hooks tree. Docker-wrapper installs keep using the shell wrapper's `upgrade` (image pull); package-managed installs (Homebrew, AUR, …) are refused
      auth                        Manage optional upstream LLM provider authentication
      user                        Manage human users and their project grants. All subcommands require the root bearer token. API keys live under `api-key`
      api-key                     Manage native `aim_` API credentials. All subcommands require the root bearer token and `[auth].token_pepper`
      project                     Project settings. `project access` sets a project `open` (any user) or `restricted` (root and grant holders) (#708). Requires the root bearer token
      profile                     Inspect and curate the cross-project profile: how you usually work, delivered to every project as defaults below the repository's rules file. Requires the root bearer token on a multi-user server
      server                      Manage local server profiles, which a repository's `.ai-memory.toml` selects with `server = "<name>"` to route its hook capture, and the project-scoped commands run there, to a different ai-memory server
      completions                 Print a shell-completion script to stdout. Generated from this binary's own command tree, so it never drifts from the real CLI surface. See `docs/shell-completions.md` for install paths
      help                        Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

    ---
    name: impeccable
    description: "Use when the user wants to design, redesign, shape, critique, audit, polish, simplify, harden, animate, or otherwise improve a frontend interface: websites, landing pages, dashboards, product UI, components, forms, settings, onboarding, empty states, and native iOS or Android screens. Handles UX review, hierarchy, layout, typography, color, motion, UX copy, accessibility, responsive behavior, edge cases, and capturing or documenting design systems (DESIGN.md, tokens). Also for making bland UI bolder, loud UI quieter, or ambitious visual effects. Not for backend-only or non-UI tasks."
    metadata:
      version: 4.8.2
    ---
    
    This skill gives you the tools and permission to make design that earns the name out-of-distribution craft. Work as an award-winning design director would: production-grade code, a clear POV, a deep grasp of what the client and its users need, and exceptional craft.
    
    Core principles:
    - Finish the job. No hedging, no shortcuts: the deliverable is complete, except assets the user must provide.
    - Scale ambition to the surface's mode (see Modes). Persuade and Experience get full commitment: bold, distinct work with a point of view. On Operate and Read, ambition shows as precision and restraint, and patterns the visitor already knows earn their place.
    - Verify by looking at the rendered result at the sizes it ships at, and batch the checks: fix what one look shows before looking again, rather than screenshotting after every tweak.
    
    ## Setup
    
    1. Run `<skill-base-dir>/scripts/impeccable context` once per session, where `<skill-base-dir>` is the directory that contains this SKILL.md (the skill folder, not a plugin root two levels above it); keep cwd at the user's project. That base directory resolves every `.agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/impeccable <verb>` command in this skill and its references, and `.agents/skills/impeccable/scripts` is the fallback only when the runtime reports no base directory. On a Windows shell without `sh`, call `.agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/impeccable.cmd` instead. The launcher runs a self-contained binary that ships next to it or is downloaded once on first run; no Node or other runtime is required. Pass a named source file or route as `--target <path>`. It loads PRODUCT.md, DESIGN.md, the matching surface brief, and native-platform guidance when applicable; follow its directives and do not rerun it.
    2. Load the request's playbook: its Commands-table reference for an explicit/implied sub-command, or [reference/new-work.md](reference/new-work.md) for a new surface or replacement visual world. Inspect target and incumbent visual truth before editing. When the app cannot run, start with committed visual-regression goldens or screenshot fixtures; verify target and freshness against current tokens, CSS, components, or assets, resolve conflicts, and compare theme/variant captures.
    3. After resolving analysis and direction, read [reference/craft-floor.md](reference/craft-floor.md) immediately before any UI edit, including small refinements. It carries the quality floor, the absolute bans, and the reflexes no detector catches. Do not load it for planning-only work.
    
    **Launcher unavailable:** On refusal or failure, send a separate message **before the next tool call**: “Context loading did not run; I’ll read the existing project context directly.” Then read existing PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md without inventing missing context, follow applicable steps 2–3, including reading [reference/craft-floor.md](reference/craft-floor.md) immediately before any UI edit, and continue through permitted tools. This applies to planning and editing; launcher failure alone does not block either.
    
    ## How to design
    
    - **The brief wins.** Honor pinned aesthetics, eras, materials, fonts, and palettes even when they conflict with a saturated-pattern warning. Redirecting a clear brief toward your taste is failure.
    - **Refinement preserves; redesign replaces.** Refinement keeps the incumbent identity, behavior, copy, and everything outside scope. Ask before replacing factual copy or adding claims. Redesign keeps product truth, content, function, native affordances, and constraints, but treats the old look as evidence and anti-reference; choose a replacement world in new-work and replace DESIGN.md. Never split the difference into polish on the discarded look.
    - **Loaded symbols stay out of the decoration.** A subject's world does not license emblems tied to militarism, supremacy, or hate movements as motifs, badges, or ornament, such as the Rising Sun flag's rays, the Confederate battle flag, or Nazi-era insignia and their stylised variants; reach for that world's neutral forms instead. Content that documents such a symbol as fact stays as it is.
    - **Visual authority is evidence, not a filename.** Missing DESIGN.md alone does not make a project greenfield; new-work decides whether to preserve, expand, or replace the incumbent world.
    
    ## Modes
    
    The mode names what the visitor's success looks like on this surface.
    
    - **Persuade:** the visitor decides and acts; design is the product. Landing pages, marketing, campaigns, pricing. Earn attention and action. Ship real imagery when the brief needs it; follow the committed world, not category habit.
    - **Operate:** the visitor completes a task. App UI, dashboards, editors, admin, settings, tools. Scanability, consistency, native expectations, and the real usage scene outrank expression. Brand lives in precise details.
    - **Read:** the visitor understands something. Docs, articles, guides, help, changelogs. Structure for comprehension, then make the reading experience worth staying in.
    - **Experience:** the visitor is inside the work itself. Portfolios, galleries, showcases. Let the artifact lead from the first viewport; the interface recedes.
    
    Choose the mode from the requested surface, not the product, and persist it only in that surface brief. A tool's landing page is still Persuade; a fashion house's documentation is still Read; a docs index is Read, not Persuade. See [new-work.md](reference/new-work.md) for new surfaces and [operate.md](reference/operate.md) for deeper Operate/Read guidance.
    
    ## Commands
    
    | Command | Category | Description | Reference |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `craft [feature]` | Build | Deprecated alias for an ordinary new-work request | [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) |
    | `shape [feature]` | Build | Plan UX/UI before writing code | [reference/shape.md](reference/shape.md) |
    | `init` | Build | Capture durable product context in PRODUCT.md | [reference/init.md](reference/init.md) |
    | `document` | Build | Generate DESIGN.md from existing project code | [reference/document.md](reference/document.md) |
    | `extract [target]` | Build | Pull reusable tokens and components into design system | [reference/extract.md](reference/extract.md) |
    | `critique [target]` | Evaluate | UX design review with heuristic scoring | [reference/critique.md](reference/critique.md) |
    | `audit [target]` | Evaluate | Technical quality checks (a11y, perf, responsive) | [reference/audit.md](reference/audit.md) · native: [reference/audit.native.md](reference/audit.native.md) |
    | `polish [target]` | Refine | Final quality pass before shipping | [reference/polish.md](reference/polish.md) |
    | `bolder [target]` | Refine | Amplify safe or bland designs | [reference/bolder.md](reference/bolder.md) |
    | `quieter [target]` | Refine | Tone down aggressive or overstimulating designs | [reference/quieter.md](reference/quieter.md) |
    | `distill [target]` | Refine | Strip to essence, remove complexity | [reference/distill.md](reference/distill.md) |
    | `harden [target]` | Refine | Production-ready: errors, i18n, edge cases | [reference/harden.md](reference/harden.md) |
    | `onboard [target]` | Refine | Design first-run flows, empty states, activation | [reference/onboard.md](reference/onboard.md) |
    | `animate [target]` | Enhance | Add purposeful animations and motion | [reference/animate.md](reference/animate.md) |
    | `colorize [target]` | Enhance | Add strategic color to monochromatic UIs | [reference/colorize.md](reference/colorize.md) |
    | `typeset [target]` | Enhance | Improve typography hierarchy and fonts | [reference/typeset.md](reference/typeset.md) |
    | `layout [target]` | Enhance | Fix spacing, rhythm, and visual hierarchy | [reference/layout.md](reference/layout.md) |
    | `delight [target]` | Enhance | Add personality and memorable touches | [reference/delight.md](reference/delight.md) |
    | `overdrive [target]` | Enhance | Push past conventional limits | [reference/overdrive.md](reference/overdrive.md) |
    | `clarify [target]` | Fix | Improve UX copy, labels, and error messages | [reference/clarify.md](reference/clarify.md) |
    | `adapt [target]` | Fix | Adapt for different devices and screen sizes | [reference/adapt.md](reference/adapt.md) · native: [reference/adapt.native.md](reference/adapt.native.md) |
    | `optimize [target]` | Fix | Diagnose and fix UI performance | [reference/optimize.md](reference/optimize.md) |
    | `live` | Iterate | Visual variant mode: pick elements in the browser, iterate on alternatives | [reference/live.md](reference/live.md) |
    | `generate [n] [action] [element]` | Iterate | Variants, versions, or alternatives of a named element to choose from in the live browser; no manual picking | [reference/generate.md](reference/generate.md) |
    
    Routing:
    
    - **No argument:** read [routing.md](reference/routing.md) and present its context-aware menu; never auto-run a command.
    - **Explicit or clearly implied request to run a command:** load its reference (native variant on native platforms) and follow it. Ask once if two commands fit.
    - **Workflow or command-selection question:** read [Workflow questions](reference/routing.md#workflow-questions).
    - **Otherwise:** treat the request as general design work. Missing PRODUCT.md routes a new surface or replacement world through init, then new-work; a narrow refinement of existing code proceeds on the incumbent implementation as `impeccable context` directs, offering init afterward rather than blocking on it.
    - `teach` aliases `init`. `craft` is a deprecated alias for ordinary new-work and adds nothing. `shape` owns task discovery, then enters new-work only for visual-world and surface-concept decisions.
    
    After init writes PRODUCT.md, resume without rerunning `impeccable context`; init loads the native platform reference itself when the platform it recorded is `ios`, `android`, or `adaptive`.
    
    **Pin / Unpin:** `.agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/impeccable pin <pin|unpin> <command>` creates or removes a standalone `$<command>` shortcut. Report the script's result concisely; relay stderr verbatim on error.
    
    **Hooks:** `$impeccable hooks <on|off|status|ignore-rule|ignore-file|ignore-value|reset>` manages the design detector hook for this project (auto-runs the detector after UI file edits and surfaces findings). Load [reference/hooks.md](reference/hooks.md) when the user invokes it with any argument.
    
    **Doctor:** `$impeccable doctor` reports and repairs drift between this project's Impeccable artifacts (PRODUCT.md, DESIGN.md and its sidecar, config, surface briefs, the hook) and what this version reads. Load [reference/doctor.md](reference/doctor.md) when the user invokes it, or when they ask what is out of date, stale, or needs refreshing. A `CONTEXT_STALE` directive in Setup's output is the cheap subset of the same report; act on it there per its own instructions rather than running doctor unasked.
    
    **Never repair drift as a side effect of a design task.** A `CONTEXT_STALE` finding is reported, not acted on, unless the user asks. The one exception is a finding marked `auto`, which the next write to that file performs anyway.

    # Craft floor
    
    Load this after the direction is settled, and build without announcing the checklist. A pinned brief or the committed visual world overrides anything here except a line marked as a ban; your own habit does not. When the design hook is active it already enforces the mechanical checks below as you edit: act on its findings instead of re-auditing each rule.
    
    ## Verify
    
    Each of these is a check on the built result, not an intention. Run them together in each batched look at the render, not as separate screenshot trips; the checks share one render.
    
    - **Contrast:** body and placeholder text ≥4.5:1, large text ≥3:1. On colored surfaces tint secondary text from that hue or the foreground; never gray.
    - **Depth:** shadows carry an offset and a soft blur. A zero-offset colored halo is decoration.
    - **Spacing:** tight groups, generous separation, more space above a heading than below it. Read the computed values.
    - **Type:** body measure 65–75ch, display max 6rem, tracking floor -0.04em, balanced headings, obvious scale and weight steps. Run the real copy at every breakpoint and fix what overflows.
    - **Motion:** one authored moment, not scattered effects and not one identical entrance on every section. Exponential ease-out from an already-visible default. Reach past transform and opacity: blur, backdrop-filter, clip-path, mask, and shadow belong to the palette when they stay smooth.
    - **States:** hover, disabled, loading, error, empty. Plus real content, working controls, responsive composition, keyboard focus.
    - **Browser surfaces:** the parts you did not draw still carry the design. Text selection, the caret, custom scrollbars, focus rings, underline offset, and the numerals in tabular data all ship with browser defaults that belong to no design system. Theme them from the palette. This is the cheapest signal that a page was built rather than assembled, and the one models skip most reliably.
    - **Copy:** the product's own language. Controls name their action; errors name the problem and the recovery.
    - **Coverage:** every brief requirement present and findable within seconds.
    
    ## Refuse
    
    These are the category's defaults, not bans: the brief's own words can earn any of them. Reaching for one when the axis is free means you were not deciding; recognizing that means rewriting the element, not softening it.
    
    Page scaffolds:
    
    - Same-size cards of icon plus heading plus text as the page structure. Cards are the lazy container; nested cards are always wrong.
    - The hero-metric template: big number, small label, supporting stats, accent.
    - A kicker or eyebrow above a heading. This one is a ban, not a default: no brief earns it back. The heading carries its own weight; delete the label and let the heading speak.
    - Section numbers (01 / 02 / 03) unless the sequence itself carries information the reader needs.
    - A modal for a task that needs neither interruption nor protected focus.
    
    Surface habits:
    
    - Gradient text. Emphasis comes from weight or size.
    - Glass and blur as decoration rather than as a specific effect.
    - A colored `border-left` or `border-right` above 1px on cards, list items, callouts, or alerts.
    - Hard offset shadows (`box-shadow: 4px 4px 0`) outside a world that is actually neobrutalist. The zero-blur block shadow is a costume, not a depth system; a world that did not choose it never earns it as a default.
    - Sparklines, progress rings, and soft-shadowed rounded rectangles standing in for content.
    - Monospace as a costume for "technical" rather than for code, data, or measurement.
    - A system display face (Impact, Arial Black, the platform sans) as the display voice of an own-world page. Source and self-host a face whose character matches the approved lettering; the closest installed font is a failure, not a fallback.
    - Unicode glyphs or emoji standing in for an icon system. Icons are drawn, from a real library or authored SVG, in one consistent stroke and weight.
    - Geometric masks standing in for organic contours. A circle, polygon, or radial-gradient cutout approximating a photographic subject's edge is the cheap version of the effect and reads worse than omitting it. Derive an alpha matte from the actual image, or produce a cut-out asset.
    - Light or dark picked by category. Pick it from the use scene: who, where, under what ambient light.
    
    - Tracking stops at -0.04em. -0.02 to -0.03em usually reads better.
    - Declare elevation once, border or shadow. A 1px border under a wide soft shadow is the ghost card. Card radii stay at 12–16px; pills are for small controls.
    - Real illustration or none. Sketch-style SVG scenes, `loose-sketch` / `doodle` class names, and `feTurbulence` grain read as amateur. This bans SVG imitating pictures, never SVG doing geometry: crisp vector shapes, diagrams, animated linework, and shader-driven effects remain first-class media. A shaded, perspectived, or figure-bearing illustration is a picture even in line-art style; geometry means shapes a session can specify exactly.
    - Backgrounds are surfaces, textured only from the subject's world. `repeating-linear-gradient` stripes and two-axis grid overlays need an actual canvas, map, blueprint, or measuring tool under them.
    - Claims and configuration come from supplied truth; label illustrative values honestly. Naming a concept and then ironizing it is not a claim.
    
    The floor holds the mechanics; it never picks the direction. With every check green, spend the page on the committed world, and when torn between refined and committed, commit.
    ✓ • 0ms

                <CheckBox x:Name="ThermalEnabled" Content="Monitorar durante os benchmarks" IsChecked="True" />
                <StackPanel Orientation="Horizontal" Spacing="12">
                  <TextBlock Text="Limite (°C)" VerticalAlignment="Center" />
                  <NumericUpDown x:Name="ThermalLimit" Minimum="60" Maximum="100" Value="90" Increment="1" Width="120" FormatString="0" />
                </StackPanel>
                <TextBlock x:Name="ThermalStateText" Text="Aguardando leitura da CPU…" Classes="sub" />
                <Button x:Name="SaveSettingsButton" Content="SALVAR CONFIGURAÇÃO" Classes="outline" Click="SavePreferences" />
                <TextBlock Text="Com o monitoramento ativo, o teste só inicia com uma leitura recente abaixo do limite." Classes="sub" />
                <Expander Header="Mais detalhes" HorizontalAlignment="Stretch" IsExpanded="False">
                  <TextBlock Text="O app solicita cancelamento quando a CPU atinge o limite, perde o sensor ou fica 5 s sem atualização. A interrupção depende da resposta dos sensores e do teste, podendo não ser imediata." Classes="sub" />
                </Expander>
              </StackPanel>
            </Border>
            <Border Classes="card">
              <StackPanel Spacing="14">
                <TextBlock Text="CPU · PATH TRACING" Classes="label" />
                <TextBlock Text="Uma cena, todos os detalhes" Classes="big" />
                <TextBlock Text="Compare a CPU com uma ou todas as threads ao renderizar a mesma imagem. A duração depende da CPU." Classes="sub" />
                <Expander Header="Mais detalhes" HorizontalAlignment="Stretch" IsExpanded="False">
                  <TextBlock Text="A cena tem 960 × 540 pixels e 1024 amostras por pixel. O teste termina ao completar a imagem." Classes="sub" />
                </Expander>
                <Image x:Name="RenderImage" Height="250" Stretch="Uniform" />
                <WrapPanel>
                  <Button x:Name="SingleButton" Content="1 THREAD" Classes="outline" Click="RunSingle" Margin="0,0,8,8" />
                  <Button x:Name="MultiButton" Content="TODAS AS THREADS" Classes="primary" Click="RunMulti" Margin="0,0,0,8" />
                </WrapPanel>
                <ContentControl x:Name="RenderRunHost">
                  <StackPanel x:Name="BenchmarkRunPanel" Spacing="12">
                <TextBlock x:Name="StatusText" Text="Pronto para iniciar." TextWrapping="Wrap" />
                <ProgressBar x:Name="RunProgress" Minimum="0" Maximum="100" />
                <Button x:Name="CancelButton" Content="CANCELAR" Classes="outline" IsEnabled="False" Click="CancelRun" />
                  </StackPanel>
                </ContentControl>
              </StackPanel>
            </Border>
            <Border Classes="card">
              <StackPanel Spacing="12">
                <TextBlock Text="CPU · SIMULAÇÃO DE JOGO" Classes="label" />
                <TextBlock Text="30 mil unidades em batalha" Classes="big" />
                <TextBlock Text="Avalie a CPU em uma simulação de física, colisões e inteligência artificial." Classes="sub" />
                <Expander Header="Mais detalhes" HorizontalAlignment="Stretch" IsExpanded="False">
                  <TextBlock Text="A simulação usa 30 mil unidades, com 2 s de aquecimento seguidos de 10 s de medição." Classes="sub" />
                </Expander>
                <Button x:Name="SimButton" Content="INICIAR SIMULAÇÃO" Classes="outline" Click="RunSimulation" />
                <ContentControl x:Name="SimulationRunHost" />
              </StackPanel>
            </Border>
            <Border Classes="card">
              <StackPanel Spacing="12">
                <TextBlock Text="MEMÓRIA RAM" Classes="label" />
                <TextBlock Text="Meça o tempo de acesso à memória e a quantidade de dados transferida por segundo." Classes="sub" />
                <TextBlock Text="Latência e largura de banda" Classes="big" />
                <TextBlock Text="A latência usa cerca de 272 MiB; o teste de banda usa cerca de 2 GiB de RAM. Execute um teste por vez, com memória disponível." Classes="sub" />
                <WrapPanel>
                  <Button x:Name="LatencyButton" Content="LATÊNCIA" Classes="outline" Click="RunLatency" Margin="0,0,8,8" />
                  <Button x:Name="BandwidthButton" Content="BANDA" Classes="outline" Click="RunBandwidth" Margin="0,0,0,8" />
                </WrapPanel>
                <ContentControl x:Name="MemoryRunHost" />
                <Button x:Name="ReleaseMemoryButton" Content="LIBERAR MEMÓRIA DOS TESTES" Classes="outline" Click="ReleaseTestMemory" />
                <TextBlock Text="Libera memória temporária do app sem apagar seus resultados. A recuperação pode ocorrer em etapas: aguarde a conclusão e a atualização do monitor do sistema antes de repetir." Classes="sub" />
                <Expander Header="Mais detalhes" HorizontalAlignment="Stretch">
                  <TextBlock Text="Após sucesso, erro ou cancelamento, uma nova limpeza pode recuperar objetos que ficaram disponíveis depois da coleta anterior. Isso não significa que vários cliques sejam sempre necessários. A ação pode causar breve pausa; o uso total de RAM pode não voltar ao valor anterior e uma nova coleta pode não reduzir o número exibido." Classes="sub" />
                </Expander>
                <TextBlock x:Name="MemoryCleanupStatusText" Classes="sub" />
              </StackPanel>
            </Border>
            <Border Classes="card">
              <StackPanel Spacing="12">
                <TextBlock Text="DISCO E CARREGAMENTO" Classes="label" />
                <TextBlock Text="Pasta do volume a testar" Classes="big" />
                <TextBox x:Name="DiskBenchmarkPath" Watermark="Caminho absoluto de uma pasta existente, por exemplo /home/usuario" />
                <TextBlock Text="Meça leitura, escrita e carregamento na pasta escolhida. Reserve 1,1 GiB livres; os testes gravam arquivos temporários." Classes="sub" />
                <Expander Header="Mais detalhes" HorizontalAlignment="Stretch" IsExpanded="False">
                  <TextBlock Text="Disco: arquivo de 512 MiB, operações sequenciais e testes aleatórios de 4 s cada. A escrita aleatória adiciona volume variável de gravação. Carregamento: pacote de 512 MiB e 1000 arquivos de 64 KiB. Os arquivos exclusivos são removidos ao terminar ou cancelar." Classes="sub" />
                </Expander>
                <TextBlock Text="O limite de temperatura monitora a CPU, não o disco." Classes="sub" />
                <Expander Header="Mais detalhes" HorizontalAlignment="Stretch" IsExpanded="False">
                  <TextBlock Text="Linux x64 com I/O direto confirmado pelo sistema de arquivos. Se indisponível, o teste informa erro. No Btrfs, apenas os arquivos temporários usam NoCoW/sem compressão; não representa arquivos Btrfs comuns. Não acessa discos brutos. Cache do dispositivo e metadados ainda podem influenciar. O limite térmico acima monitora a CPU, não o disco." Classes="sub" />
                </Expander>
                <WrapPanel>
                  <Button x:Name="DiskBenchmarkButton" Content="TESTAR DISCO" Classes="outline" Click="RunDiskBenchmark" Margin="0,0,8,8" />
                  <Button x:Name="LoadingBenchmarkButton" Content="TESTAR CARREGAMENTO" Classes="outline" Click="RunLoadingBenchmark" Margin="0,0,0,8" />
                </WrapPanel>
                <ContentControl x:Name="DiskRunHost" />
              </StackPanel>
            </Border>
            <Border Classes="card">
              <StackPanel Spacing="12">
                <TextBlock Text="REGISTRO DESTA SESSÃO" Classes="label" />
                <TextBlock Text="Acompanhe as mensagens dos testes executados desde que o app foi aberto." Classes="sub" />
                <Button x:Name="ClearSessionButton" Content="LIMPAR REGISTRO DA SESSÃO" Classes="outline" Click="ClearSessionHistory" />
                <TextBlock Text="Limpa somente o texto desta sessão. O histórico salvo e as exportações não são alterados." Classes="sub" />
                <TextBox x:Name="ResultsText" IsReadOnly="True" AcceptsReturn="True" TextWrapping="Wrap" MinHeight="90" />
              </StackPanel>
            </Border>
            <Border Classes="card">
              <StackPanel Spacing="12">
                <TextBlock Text="HISTÓRICO · ÚLTIMAS 200 EXECUÇÕES" Classes="label" />
                <TextBlock Text="Consulte e exporte os resultados e interrupções das últimas 200 execuções." Classes="sub" />
                <Expander Header="Mais detalhes" HorizontalAlignment="Stretch" IsExpanded="False">
                  <TextBlock Text="A configuração é salva pelo botão SALVAR CONFIGURAÇÃO ou ao iniciar um teste. Resultados e interrupções são gravados ao terminar. Se houver falha de gravação, use SALVAR PENDÊNCIAS / ATUALIZAR." Classes="sub" />
                </Expander>
                <TextBlock x:Name="PersistenceStatusText" Classes="sub" />
                <Button x:Name="RetrySaveButton" Content="SALVAR PENDÊNCIAS / ATUALIZAR" Classes="outline" Click="RetryPersistence" />
                <WrapPanel>
                  <Button x:Name="ExportHtmlButton" Content="EXPORTAR HTML" Classes="outline" Click="ExportHtml" Margin="0,0,8,8" />
                  <Button x:Name="ExportJsonButton" Content="EXPORTAR JSON" Classes="outline" Click="ExportJson" Margin="0,0,0,8" />
                </WrapPanel>
                <TextBlock Text="Exporta o histórico disponível, incluindo contexto do sistema e registros pendentes. Escolha onde salvar o arquivo." Classes="sub" />
                <TextBlock x:Name="ExportStatusText" Classes="sub" />
                <Button x:Name="ClearHistoryButton" Content="LIMPAR HISTÓRICO SALVO" Classes="outline" Click="RequestHistoryClear" />
                <TextBlock Text="Remove os registros salvos e pendentes usados nas próximas exportações. Arquivos já exportados e configurações são preservados." Classes="sub" />
                <StackPanel x:Name="ClearHistoryConfirmation" IsVisible="False" Spacing="8">
                  <TextBlock Text="Apagar o histórico salvo e os registros pendentes? Exporte antes se quiser guardar uma cópia." TextWrapping="Wrap" />
                  <WrapPanel>
                    <Button x:Name="ConfirmClearHistoryButton" Content="APAGAR HISTÓRICO" Classes="outline" Click="ConfirmHistoryClear" Margin="0,0,8,8" />
                    <Button Content="VOLTAR" Classes="outline" Click="DismissHistoryClear" Margin="0,0,0,8" />
                  </WrapPanel>
                </StackPanel>
                <TextBlock x:Name="HistoryClearStatusText" Classes="sub" />
                <TextBox x:Name="HistoryText" IsReadOnly="True" AcceptsReturn="True" TextWrapping="Wrap" MinHeight="140" MaxHeight="360" />
              </StackPanel>
            </Border>
          </StackPanel>
        </ScrollViewer>
      </Grid>
    </Window>
    using System.Diagnostics;
    using System.Runtime.InteropServices;
    using Avalonia;
    using Avalonia.Controls;
    using Avalonia.Interactivity;
    using Avalonia.Media.Imaging;
    using Avalonia.Platform;
    using Avalonia.Threading;
    using ResetExplode.Hardware;
    
    namespace ResetExplode;
    
    public partial class MainWindow : Window
    {
        private readonly BenchmarkRunner _runner = new();
        private readonly StackPanel _benchmarkRunPanel;
        private ContentControl _runHost;
        private readonly DispatcherTimer _timer;
        private readonly DispatcherTimer _thermalStatusTimer;
        private readonly Stopwatch _elapsed = new();
        private readonly WriteableBitmap _bitmap;
        private CancellationTokenSource? _cancellation;
        private BenchRenderer? _renderer;
        private GameSimBench? _simulation;
        private string _currentTest = "";
        private bool _closeWhenStopped;
        private bool _readyToClose;
        private bool _shutdownStarted;
    
        public MainWindow()
        {
            InitializeComponent();
            _benchmarkRunPanel = BenchmarkRunPanel;
            _runHost = RenderRunHost;
            InitializePersistence();
            MachineText.Text = $"{Environment.MachineName} · {Environment.ProcessorCount} threads lógicas · {RuntimeInformation.OSDescription}";
            _bitmap = new WriteableBitmap(new PixelSize(BenchRenderer.Width, BenchRenderer.Height),
                new Vector(96, 96), PixelFormat.Bgra8888, AlphaFormat.Opaque);
            RenderImage.Source = _bitmap;
            _renderer = new BenchRenderer();
            Array.Fill(_renderer.Pixels, unchecked((int)0xFF000000));
            RefreshBitmap();
            _renderer = null;
            _timer = new DispatcherTimer { Interval = TimeSpan.FromMilliseconds(200) };
            _timer.Tick += (_, _) => RefreshProgress();
            _thermalStatusTimer = new DispatcherTimer { Interval = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(1) };
            _thermalStatusTimer.Tick += (_, _) => UpdateThermalStatus();
            _thermalStatusTimer.Start();
            UpdateThermalStatus();
            Closing += OnClosing;
            Opened += async (_, _) => { StartTelemetry(); StartPowerMonitoring(); await Task.WhenAll(StartStorageRefresh(), StartSerialRefresh(), StartNetworkRefresh(), StartGpuDiscovery()); };
            Closed += (_, _) => { StopTelemetry(); _storageCancellation.Dispose(); _serialCancellation.Dispose(); _networkCancellation.Dispose(); _powerCancellation.Dispose(); _stabilityCancellation.Dispose(); _timer.Stop(); _thermalStatusTimer.Stop(); RenderImage.Source = null; _bitmap.Dispose(); };
        }
    
        internal void ActivateFromAnotherInstance()
        {
            if (_closeWhenStopped) return;
            if (WindowState == WindowState.Minimized) WindowState = WindowState.Normal;
            // Respeitar diálogos modais em andamento.
            var dialog = OwnedWindows.FirstOrDefault(window => window.IsVisible);
            (dialog ?? this).Activate();
        }
    
        private async void RunSingle(object? sender, RoutedEventArgs e) => await RunRenderAsync(1);
        private async void RunMulti(object? sender, RoutedEventArgs e) => await RunRenderAsync(Environment.ProcessorCount);

    #!/usr/bin/env bash
    set -euo pipefail
    
    linux_dir="$(cd -- "$(dirname -- "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
    case "${1:-}" in
      "")
        dotnet build "$linux_dir/ResetExplode.csproj" -c Release --disable-build-servers -m:1
        dotnet build "$linux_dir/SmokeTests/ResetExplode.SmokeTests.csproj" -c Release --disable-build-servers -m:1
        dotnet run --project "$linux_dir/SmokeTests/ResetExplode.SmokeTests.csproj" -c Release --no-build
        ;;
      --offline-net8)
        # Compila os projetos originais e suas referências; não inclui Avalonia.
        validation_dir="$(mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/tmp}/reset-validation.XXXXXXXX")"
        trap 'rm -rf -- "$validation_dir"' EXIT
        cat > "$validation_dir/NuGet.Config" <<'XML'
    <configuration><packageSources><clear /></packageSources></configuration>
    XML
        dotnet build "$linux_dir/SmokeTests/ResetExplode.SmokeTests.csproj" -c Release \
          --disable-build-servers -m:1 --configfile "$validation_dir/NuGet.Config" \

        private void ShowSystem(object? sender, RoutedEventArgs e) => SelectSystemPage(true);
        private void ShowBench(object? sender, RoutedEventArgs e) => SelectSystemPage(false);
    
        private void SelectSystemPage(bool system)
        {
            SystemPage.IsVisible = system;
            BenchPage.IsVisible = !system;
            SystemNav.Classes.Set("primary", system);
            SystemNav.Classes.Set("outline", !system);
            BenchNav.Classes.Set("primary", !system);
            BenchNav.Classes.Set("outline", system);

        private void StartTelemetry()
        {
            if (_telemetryCancellation != null || _telemetryStopped) return;
            var cancellation = new CancellationTokenSource();
            _telemetryCancellation = cancellation;
            // Um único loop: nunca sobrepor consultas, mesmo quando um driver responde lentamente.
            _telemetryTask = Task.Run(async () =>
            {
                var token = cancellation.Token;
                try
                {
                    var files = new LinuxFiles();
                    using var monitor = new LinuxSensorMonitor(files, new LinuxNvml());
                    var system = new LinuxSystemInfoCollector(files).Collect();

                    {
                        if (!_telemetryStopped)
                        {
                            SystemEntries.ItemsSource = system.Entries;
                            ApplySystemOverview(system);
                            _cpuDescription = system.Entries.FirstOrDefault(e => e.Name == "Processador")?.Value ?? "Indisponível";
                        }
                    });
                    while (!token.IsCancellationRequested)
                    {
                        var snapshot = monitor.Read(token);
                        // Publicar antes de aguardar a UI; o watchdog não depende do dispatcher.
                        Volatile.Write(ref _thermalReading,
                            new CpuTemperatureReading(snapshot.CpuTemperature, snapshot.CollectionStartedTimestamp));
                        await Dispatcher.UIThread.InvokeAsync(() =>
                        {
                            if (!_telemetryStopped) ApplySnapshot(snapshot);
                        });
                        await Task.Delay(TimeSpan.FromSeconds(1), token);
                    }
                }
                catch (OperationCanceledException) when (token.IsCancellationRequested) { }
                catch (Exception ex)
                {
                    Volatile.Write(ref _thermalReading, null);
                    if (!token.IsCancellationRequested)
                        await Dispatcher.UIThread.InvokeAsync(() =>

    using Avalonia.Interactivity;
    using ResetExplode.Hardware;
    
    namespace ResetExplode;
    
    public partial class MainWindow
    {
        private StressProgress? _stressProgress;
        private double _stressDurationSeconds;
    
        private async void RunStress(object? sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
        {
            if (_cancellation != null || _closeWhenStopped || _exporting || _cleaningMemory || _clearingHistory ||
                _latencyCancellation != null || _throughputCancellation != null || _dnsCancellation != null) return;
            bool cpu = StressCpu.IsChecked == true, ram = StressRam.IsChecked == true;
            if (!cpu && !ram)
            {
                StressResultText.Text = "Selecione CPU e/ou RAM para iniciar.";
                return;
            }
            long bytes = ram ? (long)(StressMemory.Value ?? 256) * StressTest.MiB : 0;
            var duration = TimeSpan.FromMinutes((double)(StressMinutes.Value ?? 5));
            _stressDurationSeconds = duration.TotalSeconds;
            StressResultText.Text = "Aguardando pré-checagem térmica e de memória…";
            try
            {
                await RunAsync("cpu-ram-stress", "Stress de CPU/RAM", ct =>
                {
                    if (ram)
                    {
                        var available = LinuxSystemInfoCollector.ReadMemory(new LinuxFiles()).AvailableBytes;
                        long runtimeBudget = GC.GetGCMemoryInfo().TotalAvailableMemoryBytes - GC.GetTotalMemory(false);
                        StressTest.ValidateMemory(bytes, available, runtimeBudget);
                    }
                    var result = StressTest.Run(cpu, bytes, duration, p => Volatile.Write(ref _stressProgress, p), ct);
                    string memory = !ram ? "RAM não selecionada" : result.MemoryPasses == 0
                        ? "RAM inconclusiva: nenhuma passada completa" : $"RAM: {result.MemoryPasses} passada(s) sem divergência observada";
                    return new BenchmarkMeasurement($"Carga encerrada após {result.Seconds:N1} s · {result.CpuThreads} thread(s) CPU · {memory}. Não comprova estabilidade.",
                        null, new() { ["seconds"] = result.Seconds, ["cpuThreads"] = result.CpuThreads,
                            ["allocatedBytes"] = result.AllocatedBytes, ["memoryPasses"] = result.MemoryPasses });
                }, new() { ["cpu"] = cpu.ToString(), ["memoryBytes"] = bytes.ToString(),
                    ["durationSeconds"] = duration.TotalSeconds.ToString(System.Globalization.CultureInfo.InvariantCulture) },
                    mandatoryThermalLimit: (double)(StressThermalLimit.Value ?? 90));
            }
            finally
            {
                Volatile.Write(ref _stressProgress, null);
            }
        }
    }
    using System.Runtime.InteropServices;
    using Avalonia.Interactivity;
    
    namespace ResetExplode;
    
    public partial class MainWindow
    {
        private LocalPersistence? _store;
        private LinuxSettings? _lastSavedSettings;
        private List<BenchmarkHistoryEntry> _history = new();
        private readonly List<BenchmarkHistoryEntry> _pendingHistory = new();
        private Task _persistenceWork = Task.CompletedTask;
        private string? _settingsNotice, _historyNotice;
        private string _cpuDescription = "Indisponível";
    
        private void InitializePersistence()
        {
            try
            {
                _store = new LocalPersistence(XdgPaths.ForCurrentUser());
                var settings = _store.LoadSettings();
                ThermalEnabled.IsChecked = settings.Value.ThermalEnabled;
                ThermalLimit.Value = (decimal)settings.Value.ThermalLimit;
                _settingsNotice = settings.Notice;
                var history = _store.LoadHistory();
                _history = history.Value.Entries;
                _historyNotice = history.Notice;
            }
            catch (Exception ex) { _settingsNotice = $"Armazenamento indisponível: {ex.Message}"; }
            RenderHistory();
            UpdatePersistenceStatus();
        }
    
        private LinuxSettings CurrentSettings() => new()
        {
            ThermalEnabled = ThermalEnabled.IsChecked == true,
            ThermalLimit = (double)(ThermalLimit.Value ?? (decimal)CpuThermalPolicy.DefaultLimit)
        };
    
        private async void SavePreferences(object? sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
        {
            if (_closeWhenStopped || _cancellation != null || _exporting || _cleaningMemory || _clearingHistory) return;
            await QueuePersistenceAsync(CurrentSettings(), forceSettings: true);
        }
    
        private async void RetryPersistence(object? sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
        {
            if (_closeWhenStopped || _cancellation != null || _exporting || _cleaningMemory || _clearingHistory) return;
            await QueuePersistenceAsync(CurrentSettings(), forceSettings: true, reloadHistory: true);
        }
    
        private Task QueuePersistenceAsync(LinuxSettings settings, bool forceSettings = false, bool reloadHistory = false)
        {
            Task previous = _persistenceWork;
            return _persistenceWork = SaveAfterAsync(previous, settings, forceSettings, reloadHistory);
        }
    
        private async Task SaveAfterAsync(Task previous, LinuxSettings settings, bool forceSettings, bool reloadHistory)
        {
            await previous;
            if (_store == null) { UpdatePersistenceStatus(); return; }
            if (forceSettings || settings != _lastSavedSettings)
            {
                try
                {
                    _settingsNotice = await Task.Run(() => _store.SaveSettings(settings));
                    _lastSavedSettings = settings;
                }
                catch (Exception ex) { _settingsNotice = $"Configuração não salva: {ex.Message}"; }
            }
            if (_pendingHistory.Count > 0)
            {
                var batch = _pendingHistory.ToArray();
                try
                {
                    var saved = await Task.Run(() => _store.AppendHistory(batch));
                    _history = saved.Value.Entries;
                    var ids = batch.Select(e => e.Id).ToHashSet();
                    _pendingHistory.RemoveAll(e => ids.Contains(e.Id));
                    _historyNotice = saved.Notice;
                }
                catch (Exception ex) { _historyNotice = $"Histórico não salvo; mantido nesta sessão: {ex.Message}"; }
            }
            else if (reloadHistory)
            {
                var loaded = await Task.Run(_store.LoadHistory);
                if (loaded.Notice == null) _history = loaded.Value.Entries;
                _historyNotice = loaded.Notice;
            }
            RenderHistory();
            UpdatePersistenceStatus();
        }
    
        private async Task RecordBenchmarkAsync(BenchmarkHistoryEntry entry, LinuxSettings settings)
        {
            _pendingHistory.Add(entry);
            RenderHistory();
            await QueuePersistenceAsync(settings);
        }
    
        private Dictionary<string, string> BenchmarkContext(Dictionary<string, string>? parameters)
        {
            var context = parameters == null ? new Dictionary<string, string>() : new Dictionary<string, string>(parameters);
            context["cpu"] = _cpuDescription;
            context["os"] = RuntimeInformation.OSDescription;
            context["architecture"] = RuntimeInformation.ProcessArchitecture.ToString();
            context["runtime"] = RuntimeInformation.FrameworkDescription;
            context["algorithmBase"] = "Windows v0.16";
            return context;
        }
    
        private void RenderHistory()
        {
            var pendingIds = _pendingHistory.Select(e => e.Id).ToHashSet();
            var rows = _pendingHistory.Concat(_history).DistinctBy(e => e.Id)
                .OrderByDescending(e => e.Date).Take(LocalPersistence.MaximumEntries);
            HistoryText.Text = string.Join(Environment.NewLine + Environment.NewLine, rows.Select(e =>
            {
                string state = e.Outcome switch
                {
                    BenchmarkOutcome.Completed => "CONCLUÍDO",
                    BenchmarkOutcome.NotStarted => "NÃO INICIADO",
                    BenchmarkOutcome.ThermalStopped => "INTERROMPIDO",
                    BenchmarkOutcome.Cancelled => "CANCELADO",
                    _ => "ERRO"
                };
                string pending = pendingIds.Contains(e.Id) ? " · NÃO SALVO" : "";
                return $"{e.Date.ToLocalTime():dd/MM/yyyy HH:mm:ss} · {e.Name} · {state}{pending}\n{e.Summary}";
            }));
        }
    
        private void UpdatePersistenceStatus()
        {
            string notices = string.Join(Environment.NewLine, new[] { _settingsNotice, _historyNotice }
                .Where(s => !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(s)));
            PersistenceStatusText.Text = notices.Length > 0 ? notices
                : $"Armazenamento local pronto · {_history.Count} registro(s) · {_pendingHistory.Count} pendente(s).";
        }
    }

O tamanho pedido deve caber em um quarto da memória disponível observada e do orçamento do runtime; essa checagem não reserva RAM nem garante disponibilidade futura. O prazo inclui alocação. Passadas incompletas não contam e zero divergências não comprova estabilidade. Cancelar ou fechar aguarda os workers.
