            StatusText.Text = $"{_currentTest} · {_elapsed.Elapsed.TotalSeconds:N0} s";
            var disk = Volatile.Read(ref _diskProgress);
            var stress = Volatile.Read(ref _stressProgress);
            var gpu = Volatile.Read(ref _gpuProgress);
            if (gpu != null)
            {
                StatusText.Text += $" · {gpu.Frames} frames · GPU {gpu.Temperature:N0} °C";
                RunProgress.IsIndeterminate = false;
                RunProgress.Value = Math.Min(100, 100 * gpu.Seconds / _gpuDurationSeconds);
            }
            else if (stress != null)
            {
                StatusText.Text += $" · {stress.AllocatedBytes / StressTest.MiB} MiB RAM · {stress.MemoryPasses} passada(s)";
                RunProgress.IsIndeterminate = false;
                RunProgress.Value = Math.Min(100, 100 * stress.Seconds / _stressDurationSeconds);
            }
            else if (disk != null)
            {
                StatusText.Text += $" · {disk.Phase}";
                RunProgress.IsIndeterminate = false;
                RunProgress.Value = 100 * disk.Fraction;
            }
            else if (_renderer != null)
            {
                RefreshBitmap();
                RunProgress.Value = 100.0 * _renderer.BucketsDone / Math.Max(1, _renderer.BucketsTotal);
            }
            else if (_simulation != null)
            {
                RunProgress.Value = 100 * _simulation.Progress;
                StatusText.Text += _elapsed.Elapsed.TotalSeconds < 2 ? " · aquecimento" : $" · {_simulation.CurrentFps:N1} FPS";
            }
        }
    
        private void RefreshBitmap()
        {
            if (_renderer == null) return;
            using var framebuffer = _bitmap.Lock();
            // O stride pode ser maior que a largura em bytes, conforme o backend.
            for (int y = 0; y < BenchRenderer.Height; y++)
                Marshal.Copy(_renderer.Pixels, y * BenchRenderer.Width,
                    IntPtr.Add(framebuffer.Address, y * framebuffer.RowBytes), BenchRenderer.Width);
            RenderImage.InvalidateVisual();
        }
    
        private async void OnClosing(object? sender, WindowClosingEventArgs e)
        {
            if (_readyToClose) return;
            e.Cancel = true;
            _closeWhenStopped = true;
            if (_cancellation != null) RequestCancellation();
            else await FinishCloseAsync();
        }
    
        private async Task FinishCloseAsync()
        {
            if (_shutdownStarted) return;
            _shutdownStarted = true;
            StopTelemetry();
            _storageCancellation.Cancel();
            _serialCancellation.Cancel();
            _networkCancellation.Cancel();
            _powerCancellation.Cancel();
            _stabilityCancellation.Cancel();
            await _powerLoop;
            await _powerWork;
            await _gpuDiscoveryWork;
            await _stabilityWork;
            await _stabilitySessionWork;
            await _kernelLogWork;
            await _storageWork;
            await _serialWork;
            await _networkWork;
            await _latencyWork;
            await _throughputWork;
            await _dnsWork;
            await _smartWork;
            // A UI continua processando os callbacks pendentes até a coleta liberar a NVML.
            await _telemetryTask;
            await _persistenceWork;
            await _exportWork;
            await _memoryCleanupWork;
            await _historyClearWork;
            _readyToClose = true;
            Close();
        }
    }

    using System.Diagnostics;
    using ResetExplode.Hardware;
    
    internal static class GpuThermalChecks
    {
        public static void Run()
        {
            GpuSelectionChecks.Run();
            const string pci = "0000:01:00.0";
            var guard = new GpuThermalGuard(pci, 80);
            GpuTemperature Reading(double? value = 50, string? error = null) => new(pci, value, Stopwatch.GetTimestamp(), error);
            void Refuse(Action action)
            {
                try { action(); }
                catch (Exception ex) when (ex is InvalidOperationException or ArgumentException) { return; }
                throw new Exception("Proteção GPU aceitou condição inválida.");
            }
            guard.Check(Reading());
            guard.Check(Reading(79.9));
            foreach (double? value in new double?[] { null, double.NaN, double.PositiveInfinity, -1, 80, 120, 151 })
                Refuse(() => guard.Check(Reading(value)));
            Refuse(() => guard.Check(Reading() with { PciAddress = "0000:00:02.0" }));
            Refuse(() => guard.Check(Reading(error: "Sensor falhou")));
            Refuse(() => guard.Check(Reading() with { StartedTimestamp = Stopwatch.GetTimestamp() - 3 * Stopwatch.Frequency }));
            Refuse(() => guard.Check(Reading() with { StartedTimestamp = Stopwatch.GetTimestamp() + Stopwatch.Frequency }));
            Refuse(() => guard.Check(Reading() with { StartedTimestamp = 0 }));
            foreach (string address in new[] { "", "GPU1", "0000:01:20.0", "0000:01:00.8", "0000:01:00.0\n" })
                Refuse(() => new GpuThermalGuard(address, 80));
            foreach (double limit in new[] { double.NaN, double.PositiveInfinity, 59, 91 })
                Refuse(() => new GpuThermalGuard(pci, limit));
            Refuse(() => VulkanGpuRenderer.Run(pci, TimeSpan.Zero));
            Refuse(() => VulkanGpuRenderer.Run(pci, TimeSpan.FromMinutes(6)));
            using var cancellation = new CancellationTokenSource(); cancellation.Cancel();
            try { VulkanGpuRenderer.Run(pci, TimeSpan.FromSeconds(1), cancellation: cancellation.Token); throw new Exception("Pré-cancelamento ignorado."); }
            catch (OperationCanceledException) { }
            Console.WriteLine("OK: proteção GPU por PCI, ausência/falha/idade do sensor, limite térmico, parâmetros e pré-cancelamento sem carga real.");
        }
    }

    layout(push_constant) uniform Params { float time; float aspect; float shells; float iterations; } p;
    layout(location=0) in vec3 normal;

    layout(location=2) in float shell;
    layout(location=0) out vec4 color;
    void main() {
        vec2 h = fract(floor(uv*vec2(900,380))*vec2(123.34,456.21));
        h += dot(h,h+45.32);
        float noise = fract(h.x*h.y);
        vec4 a=vec4(noise,uv,shell), b=a.wzyx+0.5;
        for(int k=0;k<int(p.iterations);k++) {
            a=fma(a,vec4(1.0001),b*0.0003);
            b=fma(b,vec4(0.9997),a*0.0002);
        }
        float acc=fract(dot(a,b));
        if(shell>0 && noise+acc*1e-6<shell) discard;
        float light=max(dot(normalize(normal),normalize(vec3(-0.4,0.8,-0.5))),0)*0.75+0.25;
        float c=light*mix(0.18,1.0,shell)*(0.9+0.1*acc);
        color=vec4(vec3(c),mix(1.0,0.55,shell));
    }

    layout(push_constant) uniform Params { float time; float aspect; float shells; float iterations; } p;
    layout(location=0) out vec3 normal;
    layout(location=1) out vec2 uv;
    layout(location=2) out float shell;
    void main() {
        const ivec2 corners[6] = ivec2[6](ivec2(0,0),ivec2(1,0),ivec2(0,1),ivec2(0,1),ivec2(1,0),ivec2(1,1));
        int cell = gl_VertexIndex / 6;

        uv = vec2(ij) / vec2(160,64);

        shell = float(gl_InstanceIndex) / p.shells;
        vec3 v = vec3(cos(a.x),0,sin(a.x)) + n*(0.42+0.2*shell);
        float angle = p.time*0.6;
        mat3 ry = mat3(cos(angle),0,-sin(angle), 0,1,0, sin(angle),0,cos(angle));
        float tilt = 0.55+0.25*sin(p.time*0.35);
        mat3 rx = mat3(1,0,0, 0,cos(tilt),sin(tilt), 0,-sin(tilt),cos(tilt));
        v = rx*ry*v;
        v.y -= 0.1*shell*shell;
        normal = normalize(rx*ry*n);
        float z = v.z+3.5;
        gl_Position = vec4(v.x*2.414/p.aspect, -v.y*2.414, (z-0.1)*50.0/49.9, z);
    }
    # Craft floor
    
    Load this after the direction is settled, and build without announcing the checklist. A pinned brief or the committed visual world overrides anything here except a line marked as a ban; your own habit does not. When the design hook is active it already enforces the mechanical checks below as you edit: act on its findings instead of re-auditing each rule.
    
    ## Verify
    
    Each of these is a check on the built result, not an intention. Run them together in each batched look at the render, not as separate screenshot trips; the checks share one render.
    
    - **Contrast:** body and placeholder text ≥4.5:1, large text ≥3:1. On colored surfaces tint secondary text from that hue or the foreground; never gray.
    - **Depth:** shadows carry an offset and a soft blur. A zero-offset colored halo is decoration.
    - **Spacing:** tight groups, generous separation, more space above a heading than below it. Read the computed values.
    - **Type:** body measure 65–75ch, display max 6rem, tracking floor -0.04em, balanced headings, obvious scale and weight steps. Run the real copy at every breakpoint and fix what overflows.
    - **Motion:** one authored moment, not scattered effects and not one identical entrance on every section. Exponential ease-out from an already-visible default. Reach past transform and opacity: blur, backdrop-filter, clip-path, mask, and shadow belong to the palette when they stay smooth.
    - **States:** hover, disabled, loading, error, empty. Plus real content, working controls, responsive composition, keyboard focus.
    - **Browser surfaces:** the parts you did not draw still carry the design. Text selection, the caret, custom scrollbars, focus rings, underline offset, and the numerals in tabular data all ship with browser defaults that belong to no design system. Theme them from the palette. This is the cheapest signal that a page was built rather than assembled, and the one models skip most reliably.
    - **Copy:** the product's own language. Controls name their action; errors name the problem and the recovery.
    - **Coverage:** every brief requirement present and findable within seconds.
    
    ## Refuse
    
    These are the category's defaults, not bans: the brief's own words can earn any of them. Reaching for one when the axis is free means you were not deciding; recognizing that means rewriting the element, not softening it.
    
    Page scaffolds:
    
    - Same-size cards of icon plus heading plus text as the page structure. Cards are the lazy container; nested cards are always wrong.
    - The hero-metric template: big number, small label, supporting stats, accent.
    - A kicker or eyebrow above a heading. This one is a ban, not a default: no brief earns it back. The heading carries its own weight; delete the label and let the heading speak.
    - Section numbers (01 / 02 / 03) unless the sequence itself carries information the reader needs.
    - A modal for a task that needs neither interruption nor protected focus.
    
    Surface habits:
    
    - Gradient text. Emphasis comes from weight or size.
    - Glass and blur as decoration rather than as a specific effect.
    - A colored `border-left` or `border-right` above 1px on cards, list items, callouts, or alerts.
    - Hard offset shadows (`box-shadow: 4px 4px 0`) outside a world that is actually neobrutalist. The zero-blur block shadow is a costume, not a depth system; a world that did not choose it never earns it as a default.
    - Sparklines, progress rings, and soft-shadowed rounded rectangles standing in for content.
    - Monospace as a costume for "technical" rather than for code, data, or measurement.
    - A system display face (Impact, Arial Black, the platform sans) as the display voice of an own-world page. Source and self-host a face whose character matches the approved lettering; the closest installed font is a failure, not a fallback.
    - Unicode glyphs or emoji standing in for an icon system. Icons are drawn, from a real library or authored SVG, in one consistent stroke and weight.
    - Geometric masks standing in for organic contours. A circle, polygon, or radial-gradient cutout approximating a photographic subject's edge is the cheap version of the effect and reads worse than omitting it. Derive an alpha matte from the actual image, or produce a cut-out asset.
    - Light or dark picked by category. Pick it from the use scene: who, where, under what ambient light.
    
    - Tracking stops at -0.04em. -0.02 to -0.03em usually reads better.
    - Declare elevation once, border or shadow. A 1px border under a wide soft shadow is the ghost card. Card radii stay at 12–16px; pills are for small controls.
    - Real illustration or none. Sketch-style SVG scenes, `loose-sketch` / `doodle` class names, and `feTurbulence` grain read as amateur. This bans SVG imitating pictures, never SVG doing geometry: crisp vector shapes, diagrams, animated linework, and shader-driven effects remain first-class media. A shaded, perspectived, or figure-bearing illustration is a picture even in line-art style; geometry means shapes a session can specify exactly.
    - Backgrounds are surfaces, textured only from the subject's world. `repeating-linear-gradient` stripes and two-axis grid overlays need an actual canvas, map, blueprint, or measuring tool under them.
    - Claims and configuration come from supplied truth; label illustrative values honestly. Naming a concept and then ironizing it is not a claim.
    
    The floor holds the mechanics; it never picks the direction. With every check green, spend the page on the committed world, and when torn between refined and committed, commit.

        using System.Runtime.InteropServices;
        
        namespace ResetExplode.Hardware;
        
        /// <summary>Owned snapshot of a completed frame, tightly packed RGBA8, top row first.</summary>
        public sealed class GpuPreviewFrame
        {
            private readonly byte[] _pixels;
            public int Width { get; }
            public int Height { get; }
            public long FrameNumber { get; }
            public double Seconds { get; }
            public ReadOnlyMemory<byte> Pixels => _pixels;
        
            public GpuPreviewFrame(int width, int height, long frameNumber, double seconds, ReadOnlySpan<byte> pixels)
            {
                if (width <= 0 || height <= 0 || width > 4096 || height > 4096 || pixels.Length != checked(width * height * 4))
                    throw new ArgumentException("Dimensões ou pixels inválidos para a prévia GPU.");
                if (frameNumber < 1 || !double.IsFinite(seconds) || seconds < 0)
                    throw new ArgumentException("Identificação inválida do quadro GPU.");
                Width = width; Height = height; FrameNumber = frameNumber; Seconds = seconds;
                _pixels = pixels.ToArray(); // Never expose the mapped Vulkan allocation to the UI.
            }
        
            public int NonBlackPixels()
            {
                int count = 0;
                for (int i = 0; i < _pixels.Length; i += 4)
                    if (_pixels[i] != 0 || _pixels[i + 1] != 0 || _pixels[i + 2] != 0) count++;
                return count;
            }
        
            /// <summary>The caller owns a framebuffer with at least stride * Height bytes.</summary>
            public void CopyRgbaTo(nint address, int stride)
            {
                if (address == 0 || stride < Width * 4 || (long)stride * Height > int.MaxValue)
                    throw new ArgumentException("Framebuffer inválido para a prévia GPU.");
                for (int y = 0; y < Height; y++)
                    Marshal.Copy(_pixels, y * Width * 4, address + y * stride, Width * 4);
            }
        }
        
        /// <summary>Single producer/consumer slot. A slow UI drops old frames instead of queuing them.</summary>
        public sealed class GpuPreviewBuffer
        {
            private GpuPreviewFrame? _latest;
            public void Publish(GpuPreviewFrame frame) => Interlocked.Exchange(ref _latest, frame);
            public GpuPreviewFrame? Take() => Interlocked.Exchange(ref _latest, null);
        }

        private GpuPreviewFrame ReadPixels(long frameNumber, double seconds, Action observe)
        {
            EnsureReadbackBuffer();
                Check(_vk.ResetCommandBuffer(_command, 0));
                CommandBufferBeginInfo begin = new() { SType = StructureType.CommandBufferBeginInfo, Flags = CommandBufferUsageFlags.OneTimeSubmitBit };
                Check(_vk.BeginCommandBuffer(_command, in begin));
                ImageMemoryBarrier barrier = new() { SType = StructureType.ImageMemoryBarrier,
                    SrcAccessMask = AccessFlags.ColorAttachmentWriteBit, DstAccessMask = AccessFlags.TransferReadBit,
                    OldLayout = ImageLayout.ColorAttachmentOptimal, NewLayout = ImageLayout.TransferSrcOptimal,
                    SrcQueueFamilyIndex = Vk.QueueFamilyIgnored, DstQueueFamilyIndex = Vk.QueueFamilyIgnored,
                    Image = _image, SubresourceRange = new(ImageAspectFlags.ColorBit, 0, 1, 0, 1) };
                _vk.CmdPipelineBarrier(_command, PipelineStageFlags.ColorAttachmentOutputBit, PipelineStageFlags.TransferBit,
                    0, 0, null, 0, null, 1, &barrier);
                BufferImageCopy copy = new() { ImageSubresource = new(ImageAspectFlags.ColorBit, 0, 0, 1), ImageExtent = new(_width, _height, 1) };
                _vk.CmdCopyImageToBuffer(_command, _image, ImageLayout.TransferSrcOptimal, _readbackBuffer, 1, in copy);
                // Restore the attachment layout for subsequent draws and final readback.
                ImageMemoryBarrier restore = barrier;
                restore.SrcAccessMask = AccessFlags.TransferReadBit;
                restore.DstAccessMask = AccessFlags.ColorAttachmentWriteBit;
                restore.OldLayout = ImageLayout.TransferSrcOptimal;
                restore.NewLayout = ImageLayout.ColorAttachmentOptimal;
                _vk.CmdPipelineBarrier(_command, PipelineStageFlags.TransferBit, PipelineStageFlags.ColorAttachmentOutputBit,
                    0, 0, null, 0, null, 1, &restore);
                MemoryBarrier host = new() { SType = StructureType.MemoryBarrier,
                    SrcAccessMask = AccessFlags.TransferWriteBit, DstAccessMask = AccessFlags.HostReadBit };
                _vk.CmdPipelineBarrier(_command, PipelineStageFlags.TransferBit, PipelineStageFlags.HostBit,
                    0, 1, &host, 0, null, 0, null);
                Check(_vk.EndCommandBuffer(_command));
                Check(_vk.ResetFences(_device, 1, in _fence));
                var command = _command;
                SubmitInfo submit = new() { SType = StructureType.SubmitInfo, CommandBufferCount = 1, PCommandBuffers = &command };
                observe();
                Check(_vk.QueueSubmit(_queue, 1, in submit, _fence));
            WaitForFrame(observe);
            ulong size = (ulong)_width * _height * 4;
            void* data = null;
            Check(_vk.MapMemory(_device, _readbackMemory, 0, size, 0, &data));
            try
            {
                return new((int)_width, (int)_height, frameNumber, seconds, new ReadOnlySpan<byte>(data, checked((int)size)));
            }
            finally { _vk.UnmapMemory(_device, _readbackMemory); }
        }
    
        public static GpuRenderResult Run(string pciAddress, TimeSpan duration, double thermalLimit = 80,
            CancellationToken cancellation = default, Action<GpuRenderProgress>? progress = null, RenderGpu? expectedDevice = null,
            Action<GpuPreviewFrame>? preview = null)
        {
            if (duration < TimeSpan.FromMilliseconds(100) || duration > TimeSpan.FromMinutes(5))
                throw new ArgumentOutOfRangeException(nameof(duration));
            var guard = new GpuThermalGuard(pciAddress, thermalLimit);
            cancellation.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
            var devices = Enumerate().Where(d => d.PciAddress == pciAddress && d.Hardware).ToArray();
            if (devices.Length != 1) throw new NotSupportedException("GPU selecionada ausente ou ambígua; atualize a lista.");
            if (expectedDevice != null && devices[0] != expectedDevice)
                throw new InvalidOperationException("A GPU mudou desde a seleção; atualize a lista antes de iniciar.");
            using var sensor = new LinuxGpuTemperatureSource(new LinuxFiles());
            guard.Check(sensor.Read(devices[0]));
            using var renderer = new VulkanGpuRenderer(pciAddress, 640, 360, 32);
            if (renderer.Selected.Name != devices[0].Name || renderer.Selected.VendorId != devices[0].VendorId)
                throw new InvalidOperationException("Identificação da GPU mudou; atualize a lista.");
            var clock = Stopwatch.StartNew();
            long frames = 0;
            bool submitted = false;
            double lastTemperature = 0;
            TimeSpan nextPreview = TimeSpan.Zero;
            GpuPreviewFrame? lastPreview = null;
            void Observe()
            {
                cancellation.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
                var reading = sensor.Read(renderer.Selected);
                try { guard.Check(reading); }
                catch (GpuThermalException ex) { ex.LoadStarted = submitted; throw; }
                lastTemperature = reading.Celsius!.Value;
            }
            while (clock.Elapsed < duration)
            {
                Observe();
                renderer.Draw((float)clock.Elapsed.TotalSeconds, Observe, () => submitted = true);
                frames++;
                if (preview != null && clock.Elapsed >= nextPreview)
                {
                    lastPreview = renderer.ReadPixels(frames, clock.Elapsed.TotalSeconds, Observe);
                    preview(lastPreview);
                    nextPreview = clock.Elapsed + TimeSpan.FromMilliseconds(200);
                }
                progress?.Invoke(new(frames, clock.Elapsed.TotalSeconds, lastTemperature));
            }
            Observe();
            if (frames == 0) throw new InvalidOperationException("Prazo encerrado sem frame concluído.");
            double seconds = clock.Elapsed.TotalSeconds;
            var finalFrame = lastPreview?.FrameNumber == frames ? lastPreview
                : renderer.ReadPixels(frames, seconds, Observe);
            if (preview != null && finalFrame != lastPreview) preview(finalFrame);
            int pixels = finalFrame.NonBlackPixels();
            Observe();
            if (pixels == 0) throw new InvalidOperationException("O pipeline não produziu pixels visíveis.");
            return new(renderer.Selected.Name, pciAddress, frames, seconds, pixels);
        }
    
        public void Dispose()
        {
            if (_disposed) return;
            _disposed = true;
            if (_device.Handle != 0)
            {
                _vk.DeviceWaitIdle(_device);
                if (_readbackBuffer.Handle != 0) _vk.DestroyBuffer(_device, _readbackBuffer, null);
                if (_readbackMemory.Handle != 0) _vk.FreeMemory(_device, _readbackMemory, null);
                if (_fence.Handle != 0) _vk.DestroyFence(_device, _fence, null);
                if (_pool.Handle != 0) _vk.DestroyCommandPool(_device, _pool, null);
                if (_pipeline.Handle != 0) _vk.DestroyPipeline(_device, _pipeline, null);
                if (_layout.Handle != 0) _vk.DestroyPipelineLayout(_device, _layout, null);
                if (_framebuffer.Handle != 0) _vk.DestroyFramebuffer(_device, _framebuffer, null);
                if (_pass.Handle != 0) _vk.DestroyRenderPass(_device, _pass, null);
                if (_view.Handle != 0) _vk.DestroyImageView(_device, _view, null);
                if (_image.Handle != 0) _vk.DestroyImage(_device, _image, null);
                if (_memory.Handle != 0) _vk.FreeMemory(_device, _memory, null);
                _vk.DestroyDevice(_device, null);
            }
            if (_instance.Handle != 0) _vk.DestroyInstance(_instance, null);
            _vk.Dispose();
        }
    }

        {
            Exception? stopped = null;
            // Não liberar recursos em uso pela GPU. Cancelamento impede novas submissões,
            // mas ainda espera a fence do frame já enviado (driver travado pode atrasar o retorno).
            while (true)
            {
                var result = _vk.WaitForFences(_device, 1, in _fence, true, 100_000_000);
                if (stopped == null) { try { observe(); } catch (Exception ex) { stopped = ex; } }
                if (result == Result.Timeout) continue;
                Check(result); break;
            }
            if (stopped != null) System.Runtime.ExceptionServices.ExceptionDispatchInfo.Capture(stopped).Throw();
        }
    
        private void EnsureReadbackBuffer()
        {
            if (_readbackBuffer.Handle != 0) return;
            ulong size = (ulong)_width * _height * 4;
            BufferCreateInfo bufferInfo = new() { SType = StructureType.BufferCreateInfo, Size = size,
                Usage = BufferUsageFlags.TransferDstBit, SharingMode = SharingMode.Exclusive };
            Check(_vk.CreateBuffer(_device, in bufferInfo, null, out _readbackBuffer));
            _vk.GetBufferMemoryRequirements(_device, _readbackBuffer, out var requirements);
            _vk.GetPhysicalDeviceMemoryProperties(_physical, out var properties);
            uint? type = null;
            var flags = MemoryPropertyFlags.HostVisibleBit | MemoryPropertyFlags.HostCoherentBit;
            for (uint i = 0; i < properties.MemoryTypeCount; i++)
                if ((requirements.MemoryTypeBits & (1u << (int)i)) != 0 && (properties.MemoryTypes[(int)i].PropertyFlags & flags) == flags)
                { type = i; break; }
            if (type == null) throw new NotSupportedException("Memória de leitura dos pixels indisponível.");
            MemoryAllocateInfo allocation = new() { SType = StructureType.MemoryAllocateInfo, AllocationSize = requirements.Size, MemoryTypeIndex = type.Value };
            Check(_vk.AllocateMemory(_device, in allocation, null, out _readbackMemory));
            Check(_vk.BindBufferMemory(_device, _readbackBuffer, _readbackMemory, 0));
        }
    
        private GpuPreviewFrame ReadPixels(long frameNumber, double seconds, Action observe)
        {
            EnsureReadbackBuffer();
            Check(_vk.ResetCommandBuffer(_command, 0));
            CommandBufferBeginInfo begin = new() { SType = StructureType.CommandBufferBeginInfo, Flags = CommandBufferUsageFlags.OneTimeSubmitBit };
            Check(_vk.BeginCommandBuffer(_command, in begin));
            ImageMemoryBarrier barrier = new() { SType = StructureType.ImageMemoryBarrier,
            finally
            {
                _gpuDiscovering = false;
                UpdateGpuSelection();
            }
        }
    
        private void GpuSelectionChanged(object? sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e) => UpdateGpuSelection();
        private void UpdateGpuSelection()
        {
            if (GpuSelectionText == null) return; // Eventos durante InitializeComponent.
            GpuSelectionText.Text = (GpuSelector.SelectedItem as GpuStressChoice)?.Status ?? "Selecione uma GPU. Nenhum dispositivo é escolhido automaticamente.";
            if (_cancellation == null && GpuPreviewImage != null) ResetGpuPreview();
            UpdateGpuControls();
        }
    
        private void UpdateGpuControls()
        {
            if (GpuStartButton == null) return;
            bool available = _cancellation == null && !_gpuDiscovering && !_closeWhenStopped && !_exporting &&
                !_cleaningMemory && !_clearingHistory && _latencyCancellation == null && _throughputCancellation == null && _dnsCancellation == null;
            GpuSelector.IsEnabled = GpuRefreshButton.IsEnabled = GpuMinutes.IsEnabled = GpuLimit.IsEnabled = available;
            GpuStartButton.IsEnabled = available && GpuSelector.SelectedItem is GpuStressChoice { CanStress: true };
        }
    
        private async void RunGpuStress(object? sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
        {
            if (!GpuStartButton.IsEnabled || GpuSelector.SelectedItem is not GpuStressChoice { CanStress: true } choice || choice.Device.PciAddress == null) return;
            string pci = choice.Device.PciAddress;
            double limit = (double)(GpuLimit.Value ?? 80);
            var duration = TimeSpan.FromMinutes((double)(GpuMinutes.Value ?? 1));
            _gpuDurationSeconds = duration.TotalSeconds;
            ResetGpuPreview();
            _gpuPreviewDevice = choice.Device.Name;
            GpuPreviewStatusText.Text = "Preparando a GPU; aguardando o primeiro quadro…";
            GpuPreviewPanel.BringIntoView();
            GpuResultText.Text = $"Preparando {choice.Device.Name}; verificando novamente GPU e temperatura…";
            try
            {
                await RunAsync("gpu-stress", $"Stress GPU · {choice.Device.Name}", ct =>
                {
                    var result = VulkanGpuRenderer.Run(pci, duration, limit, ct, p => Volatile.Write(ref _gpuProgress, p), choice.Device, _gpuPreview.Publish);
                    return new BenchmarkMeasurement($"{result.Device} · {result.Frames} frames em {result.Seconds:N1} s. Não comprova estabilidade.",
                        null, new() { ["frames"] = result.Frames, ["seconds"] = result.Seconds, ["nonBlackPixels"] = result.NonBlackPixels });
                }, new() { ["gpuName"] = choice.Device.Name, ["gpuType"] = choice.Device.Kind, ["gpuPci"] = pci,
                    ["durationSeconds"] = duration.TotalSeconds.ToString(System.Globalization.CultureInfo.InvariantCulture),
                    ["preview"] = "RGBA8 640x360 readback; at most 5 updates/s plus final frame; latest frame only",
                    ["methodology"] = "linux-gpu-stress-preview-v2" }, gpuThermalLimit: limit);
            }
            finally
            {
                Volatile.Write(ref _gpuProgress, null);
                RefreshGpuPreview();
                if (!_closeWhenStopped)
                    GpuPreviewStatusText.Text = _gpuPreviewFrameNumber == 0
                        ? "Teste encerrado sem imagem recebida. Consulte o resultado abaixo."
                        : $"Imagem parada · {_gpuPreviewDevice} · último quadro recebido: {_gpuPreviewFrameNumber}. Consulte o resultado abaixo.";
            }
        }
    }

        using System.Runtime.InteropServices;
        using ResetExplode.Hardware;
        
        internal static class GpuPreviewChecks
        {
            public static void Run()
            {
                byte[] source = [255, 0, 0, 255, 0, 0, 0, 255, 0, 0, 255, 255, 0, 255, 0, 255];
                var frame = new GpuPreviewFrame(2, 2, 1, 0.5, source);
                source[0] = 0;
                if (frame.Pixels.Span[0] != 255 || frame.NonBlackPixels() != 3) throw new Exception("Quadro não possui seus pixels ou conta alfa como cor.");
                var memory = Marshal.AllocHGlobal(24);
                try
                {
                    Marshal.Copy(Enumerable.Repeat((byte)77, 24).ToArray(), 0, memory, 24);
                    frame.CopyRgbaTo(memory, 12);
                    var copied = new byte[24]; Marshal.Copy(memory, copied, 0, 24);
                    if (!copied.AsSpan(0, 8).SequenceEqual(frame.Pixels.Span[..8]) ||
                        !copied.AsSpan(12, 8).SequenceEqual(frame.Pixels.Span[8..]) ||
                        copied[8] != 77 || copied[11] != 77 || copied[20] != 77 || copied[23] != 77)
                        throw new Exception("Prévia alterou canais, orientação ou padding do framebuffer.");
                }
                finally { Marshal.FreeHGlobal(memory); }
                void Refuse(Action action)
                {
                    try { action(); } catch (ArgumentException) { return; }
                    throw new Exception("Prévia aceitou entrada inválida.");
                }
                Refuse(() => new GpuPreviewFrame(2, 2, 1, 0, new byte[15]));
                Refuse(() => new GpuPreviewFrame(0, 2, 1, 0, []));
                Refuse(() => new GpuPreviewFrame(int.MaxValue, 2, 1, 0, []));
                Refuse(() => new GpuPreviewFrame(2, 2, 0, 0, source));
                Refuse(() => new GpuPreviewFrame(2, 2, 1, double.NaN, source));
                Refuse(() => frame.CopyRgbaTo(0, 8));
                Refuse(() => frame.CopyRgbaTo(1, 7));
                var buffer = new GpuPreviewBuffer();
                buffer.Publish(frame);
                var second = new GpuPreviewFrame(2, 2, 2, 0.8, source);
                buffer.Publish(second);
                if (buffer.Take() != second || buffer.Take() != null) throw new Exception("Prévia acumulou quadros ou repetiu consumo.");
                Parallel.For(0, 500, i => { buffer.Publish(i % 2 == 0 ? frame : second); buffer.Take(); });
                buffer.Take(); buffer.Publish(frame);
                if (buffer.Take() != frame || buffer.Take() != null) throw new Exception("Slot de prévia inválido após concorrência.");
                using var cancel = new CancellationTokenSource(); cancel.Cancel();
                int calls = 0;
                try { VulkanGpuRenderer.Run("0000:01:00.0", TimeSpan.FromSeconds(1), cancellation: cancel.Token, preview: _ => calls++); }
                catch (OperationCanceledException) { }
                if (calls != 0) throw new Exception("Prévia publicada após pré-cancelamento.");
                Console.WriteLine("OK: prévia GPU RGBA, propriedade dos pixels, stride, padding, limites, slot único e pré-cancelamento.");
            }
        }

        using Avalonia.Controls;
        using Avalonia.Interactivity;
        using Avalonia.Media.Imaging;
        using Avalonia.Threading;
        using ResetExplode;
        using ResetExplode.Hardware;
        AppBuilder.Configure<App>().UsePlatformDetect().SetupWithoutStarting();
        var window = new MainWindow();
        window.FindControl<Button>("BenchNav")!.RaiseEvent(new RoutedEventArgs(Button.ClickEvent));
        window.FindControl<TabControl>("BenchmarkSections")!.SelectedIndex = 3;
        var selector = window.FindControl<ComboBox>("GpuSelector")!;
        var start = window.FindControl<Button>("GpuStartButton")!;
        var status = window.FindControl<TextBlock>("GpuPreviewStatusText")!;
        var image = window.FindControl<Image>("GpuPreviewImage")!;
        var panel = window.FindControl<StackPanel>("GpuPreviewPanel")!;
        var stop = new CancellationTokenSource();
        var clock = Stopwatch.StartNew();
        var stageClock = Stopwatch.StartNew();
        int stage = 0;
        GpuStressChoice? choice = null;
        void Capture(string name) {
         using var bitmap = new RenderTargetBitmap(new PixelSize((int)window.Bounds.Width, (int)window.Bounds.Height));
         bitmap.Render(window); bitmap.Save($"/tmp/reset-bench-preview/gpu-{name}.png");
        }
        var timer = new DispatcherTimer { Interval = TimeSpan.FromMilliseconds(250) };
        window.Closed += (_, _) => { timer.Stop(); stop.Cancel(); };
        timer.Tick += (_, _) => {
         try {
         if (clock.Elapsed.TotalSeconds > 30) throw new Exception("Prazo da validação UI excedido.");
         if (stage == 0) {
          choice = (selector.ItemsSource as IEnumerable<GpuStressChoice>)?.FirstOrDefault(c => c.CanStress);
          if (choice == null) return;
          selector.SelectedItem = choice;
          start.RaiseEvent(new RoutedEventArgs(Button.ClickEvent)); stageClock.Restart(); stage = 1;
         } else if (stage == 1 && stageClock.Elapsed.TotalSeconds > 3) {
          if (image.Source == null || !status.Text!.Contains("quadro")) throw new Exception("Prévia não chegou à UI.");
          Capture("running"); ((WriteableBitmap)image.Source).Save("/tmp/reset-bench-preview/gpu-scene.png");
          Console.WriteLine(status.Text);
          window.FindControl<Button>("CancelButton")!.RaiseEvent(new RoutedEventArgs(Button.ClickEvent)); stage = 2;
         } else if (stage == 2 && start.IsEnabled) {
          if (!status.Text!.StartsWith("Imagem parada")) throw new Exception("Imagem final sem identificação de parada.");
          Capture("stopped");
          var unavailable = ((IEnumerable<GpuStressChoice>)selector.ItemsSource!).FirstOrDefault(c => !c.CanStress);
          if (unavailable != null) {
           selector.SelectedItem = unavailable;
           if (image.Source != null || start.IsEnabled) throw new Exception("Troca de GPU deixou imagem antiga ou permitiu GPU indisponível.");
          }
          selector.SelectedItem = choice;
          start.RaiseEvent(new RoutedEventArgs(Button.ClickEvent)); stageClock.Restart(); stage = 3;
         } else if (stage == 3 && stageClock.Elapsed.TotalSeconds > 2) {
          if (image.Source == null) throw new Exception("Reinício sem prévia.");
          Console.WriteLine("OK: prévia ao vivo, cancelamento, imagem parada, troca de dispositivo e reinício. Fechando durante carga.");
          stage = 4; timer.Stop(); window.Close();
         }
         } catch (Exception ex) { Console.Error.WriteLine(ex); Environment.ExitCode = 1; timer.Stop(); window.Close(); }
        };
        window.Show(); timer.Start(); Dispatcher.UIThread.MainLoop(stop.Token);
        Console.WriteLine("Janela encerrada.");

    docs/0003-funcionalidades/0015-gpu-benchmarks-e-stress.md:3:**Estado Linux em 23/11/2026:** Backend offscreen e stress com seleção multi-GPU na interface implementados; preview e benchmarks gráficos pendentes.
    docs/0003-funcionalidades/0015-gpu-benchmarks-e-stress.md:11:Windows possui GpuBurn, GpuGameBench e GpuRtBench com Direct3D. O Linux possui agora um pipeline Vulkan offscreen inicial; não é equivalente integral à referência Windows. NVML atual fornece telemetria, não renderização.
    docs/0003-funcionalidades/0015-gpu-benchmarks-e-stress.md:33:Bindings, shaders e sincronização do pipeline offscreen estão implementados. Apresentação em janela, suporte a ray tracing e semântica da pontuação permanecem pendentes. Não declarar equivalência de score antes de validar metodologia.
    docs/0003-funcionalidades/0015-gpu-benchmarks-e-stress.md:49:Diagnósticos `--gpu-devices` e `--gpu-render <PCI> <segundos>` em SmokeTests. Renderiza torus procedural offscreen 640×360, 32 camadas e 32 iterações ALU, sem janela/depth/MSAA; uma submissão por vez. Retorna frames concluídos, duração e contagem de pixels não pretos, sem score. Ctrl+C impede novas submissões e aguarda recursos em uso. Biblioteca/API/drivers indisponíveis geram erro explícito.

        p=Path('docs/0003-funcionalidades/0015-gpu-benchmarks-e-stress.md');s=p.read_text()+'''
        ## Prévia da carga real — 23/11/2026
        
        Benchmark → GPU exibe os pixels RGBA8 do torus renderizado pela placa selecionada. O backend reutiliza um buffer de leitura Vulkan coerente, aguarda a fence e publica um snapshot próprio até a cada 200 ms, mais o quadro final. A UI consome somente o snapshot mais recente, respeita o stride do bitmap e não bloqueia o worker em callbacks do dispatcher. Temperatura e cancelamento são verificados também enquanto a cópia aguarda a GPU; recursos em uso só são liberados após terminar a submissão.
        
        Ao iniciar ou trocar a GPU, a imagem anterior é limpa. Após sucesso, cancelamento ou falha, a última imagem recebida fica marcada como parada; sem quadro recebido, o estado é explícito. Fechar cancela a carga e descarta a imagem após o worker encerrar.
        
        A prévia não mede FPS de jogos. O readback periódico tem custo e integra o tempo do loop; histórico/exportações da UI identificam `linux-gpu-stress-preview-v2`, distinguindo execuções antigas sem prévia. São preservados resolução, shaders, 32 camadas, proteção térmica e ausência de score. A cópia final e inicialização ficam fora da duração reportada.
        
        Sincronização segue [exemplos oficiais Vulkan de leitura pela CPU](https://docs.vulkan.org/guide/latest/synchronization_examples.html): barreira para leitura host, fence e memória coerente; a imagem retorna ao layout de color attachment após a cópia.
        ''';p.write_text(s)
        PY

    # GPU Vulkan — atualizado em 23/11/2026
    
    ## Pela interface
    
    Em Benchmark → GPU → Stress de GPU, aguarde a detecção, selecione uma GPU, ajuste duração/limite e inicie. Nenhum dispositivo é selecionado automaticamente. Atualizar mantém a escolha por PCI ou limpa a seleção se o dispositivo desaparecer. GPU sem sensor próprio permanece na lista e explica por que não pode iniciar.
    
    - [x] Detecção real de Intel UHD e GTX 1650, seleção explícita e atualização preservando PCI.
    - [x] GTX habilitada; Intel sem sensor próprio bloqueada com motivo legível.
    - [x] Carga curta e cancelamento pela UI, bloqueios recíprocos com benchmarks e controles liberados ao terminar.
    - [x] Histórico temporário: GPU/PCI, proteção térmica GPU, cancelled e ausência de métricas parciais.
    - [x] Conteúdo visual conferido em 945×993, sem corte horizontal.
    - [ ] Conferir teclado, dropdown e layout no tamanho mínimo real (o gerenciador de janelas impediu validar 820×620).
    - [ ] Conferir fechamento da janela durante carga GPU e reabertura.
    - [ ] Concluir teste prolongado e conferir relatórios HTML/JSON pelo diálogo.
    - [ ] Validar carga real AMD/Intel com sensor hwmon próprio; por enquanto testado com fixtures.
    
    ## Executar
    
    Após compilar SmokeTests em Release, liste os dispositivos e escolha explicitamente o PCI NVIDIA reportado:
    
    ```bash
    dotnet Linux/SmokeTests/bin/Release/net8.0/ResetExplode.SmokeTests.dll --gpu-devices
    dotnet Linux/SmokeTests/bin/Release/net8.0/ResetExplode.SmokeTests.dll --gpu-render 0000:01:00.0 2
    ```
    
    O endereço acima é o observado nesta máquina, não um padrão universal. A execução gera carga real de GPU, offscreen, por 1–300 s. Limite obrigatório de 80 °C no CLI. Ctrl+C solicita cancelamento e aguarda o frame em execução; não há janela de preview nem resultado parcial exportado. Não executar simultaneamente com outros benchmarks. Exige Vulkan 1.1, driver, GPU física identificável por PCI e sensor correspondente (NVML NVIDIA ou hwmon próprio Intel/AMD). Não precisa de vulkaninfo para renderizar.
    
    ## Evidências
    
    - [x] Enumeração Vulkan nativa identificou Intel e NVIDIA com endereços PCI distintos.
    - [x] Carga curta na GTX 1650 produziu frames completos e pixels não pretos.
    - [x] Cancelamento real por Ctrl+C retornou 130, sem publicar resultado parcial; nova execução posterior concluiu normalmente.
    - [x] Testes simulados recusam sensor de outra GPU, ausente, obsoleto, inválido ou no limite térmico.
    - [x] SPIR-V passou por spirv-val com alvo Vulkan 1.1.
    - [ ] Validação visual do torus, quando houver preview.
    - [ ] Validação com camada Khronos de diagnóstico Vulkan (não disponível neste ambiente).
    - [x] Cancelamento integrado à janela; fechamento sob carga ainda aguarda conferência manual.
    
    Sem certificação de estabilidade, saturação máxima ou equivalência com Windows. Não provocar superaquecimento para validar proteção. Driver travado pode atrasar indefinidamente o retorno das funções nativas/fences; o backend não destrói recursos que ainda estejam em uso.
    
    ## Recompilar shaders
    
    SPIR-V já está versionado; glslc e spirv-val são necessários apenas para modificar/verificar shaders:
    
    ```bash
    glslc --target-env=vulkan1.1 -O Linux/Hardware/Gpu/Shaders/burn.vert -o Linux/Hardware/Gpu/Shaders/burn.vert.spv
    glslc --target-env=vulkan1.1 -O Linux/Hardware/Gpu/Shaders/burn.frag -o Linux/Hardware/Gpu/Shaders/burn.frag.spv
    spirv-val --target-env vulkan1.1 Linux/Hardware/Gpu/Shaders/burn.vert.spv
    spirv-val --target-env vulkan1.1 Linux/Hardware/Gpu/Shaders/burn.frag.spv
    ```

## Activity

    $ cat /home/ruralgiovane/.agents/skills/impeccable/reference/adapt.md; cat /home/ruralgiovane/.agents/skills/impeccable/reference/craft-floor.md; sed -n '1,130p' Linux/Views/MainWindow.axaml; sed -n '1,140p' Linux/Views/MainWindow.Telemetry.cs; git diff --stat; git log -5 --oneline
    > **Additional context needed**: target platforms/devices and usage contexts.
    
    Adapt an existing design to a different context: another screen size, device, platform, or use case. The trap is treating adaptation as scaling. The job is rethinking the experience for the new context.
    
    **Web only** (mobile web included). Native platforms (`ios` / `android` / `adaptive`) route to [adapt.native.md](adapt.native.md) instead; if the project is native, switch to it now.
    
    ---
    
    ## Assess Adaptation Challenge
    
    Understand what needs adaptation and why:
    
    1. **Identify the source context**:
       - What was it designed for originally? (Desktop web? Mobile app?)
       - What assumptions were made? (Large screen? Mouse input? Fast connection?)
       - What works well in current context?
    
    2. **Understand target context**:
       - **Device**: Mobile, tablet, desktop, TV, watch, print?
       - **Input method**: Touch, mouse, keyboard, voice, gamepad?
       - **Screen constraints**: Size, resolution, orientation?
       - **Connection**: Fast wifi, slow 3G, offline?
       - **Usage context**: On-the-go vs desk, quick glance vs focused reading?
       - **User expectations**: What do users expect on this platform?
    
    3. **Identify adaptation challenges**:
       - What won't fit? (Content, navigation, features)
       - What won't work? (Hover states on touch, tiny touch targets)
       - What's inappropriate? (Desktop patterns on mobile, mobile patterns on desktop)
    
    Adaptation is rethinking the experience for the new context, not scaling pixels.
    
    ## Plan Adaptation Strategy
    
    Create context-appropriate strategy:
    
    ### Mobile Adaptation (Desktop → Mobile)
    
    **Layout Strategy**:
    - Single column instead of multi-column
    - Vertical stacking instead of side-by-side
    - Full-width components instead of fixed widths
    - Bottom navigation instead of top/side navigation
    
    **Interaction Strategy**:
    - Touch targets 44x44px minimum (not hover-dependent)
    - Swipe gestures where appropriate (lists, carousels)
    - Bottom sheets instead of dropdowns
    - Thumbs-first design (controls within thumb reach)
    - Larger tap areas with more spacing
    
    **Content Strategy**:
    - Progressive disclosure (don't show everything at once)
    - Prioritize primary content (secondary content in tabs/accordions)
    - Shorter text (more concise)
    - Larger text (16px minimum)
    
    **Navigation Strategy**:
    - Hamburger menu or bottom navigation
    - Reduce navigation complexity
    - Sticky headers for context
    - Back button in navigation flow
    
    ### Tablet Adaptation (Hybrid Approach)
    
    **Layout Strategy**:
    - Two-column layouts (not single or three-column)
    - Side panels for secondary content
    - Master-detail views (list + detail)
    - Adaptive based on orientation (portrait vs landscape)
    
    **Interaction Strategy**:
    - Support both touch and pointer
    - Touch targets 44x44px but allow denser layouts than phone
    - Side navigation drawers
    - Multi-column forms where appropriate
    
    ### Desktop Adaptation (Mobile → Desktop)
    
    **Layout Strategy**:
    - Multi-column layouts (use horizontal space)
    - Side navigation always visible
    - Multiple information panels simultaneously
    - Fixed widths with max-width constraints (don't stretch to 4K)
    
    **Interaction Strategy**:
    - Hover states for additional information
    - Keyboard shortcuts
    - Right-click context menus
    - Drag and drop where helpful
    - Multi-select with Shift/Cmd
    
    **Content Strategy**:
    - Show more information upfront (less progressive disclosure)
    - Data tables with many columns
    - Richer visualizations
    - More detailed descriptions
    
    ### Print Adaptation (Screen → Print)
    
    **Layout Strategy**:
    - Page breaks at logical points
    - Remove navigation, footer, interactive elements
    - Black and white (or limited color)
    - Proper margins for binding
    
    **Content Strategy**:
    - Expand shortened content (show full URLs, hidden sections)
    - Add page numbers, headers, footers
    - Include metadata (print date, page title)
    - Convert charts to print-friendly versions
    
    ### Email Adaptation (Web → Email)
    
    **Layout Strategy**:
    - Narrow width (600px max)
    - Single column only
    - Inline CSS (no external stylesheets)
    - Table-based layouts (for email client compatibility)
    
    **Interaction Strategy**:
    - Large, obvious CTAs (buttons not text links)
    - No hover states (not reliable)
    - Deep links to web app for complex interactions
    
    ## Implement Adaptations
    
    Apply changes systematically:
    
    ### Responsive Breakpoints
    
    Choose appropriate breakpoints:
    - Mobile: 320px-767px
    - Tablet: 768px-1023px
    - Desktop: 1024px+
    - Or content-driven breakpoints (where design breaks)
    
    ### Layout Adaptation Techniques
    
    - **CSS Grid/Flexbox**: Reflow layouts automatically
    - **Container Queries**: Adapt based on container, not viewport
    - **`clamp()`**: Fluid sizing between min and max
    - **Media queries**: Different styles for different contexts
    - **Display properties**: Show/hide elements per context
    
    ### Touch Adaptation
    
    - Increase touch target sizes (44x44px minimum)
    - Add more spacing between interactive elements
    - Remove hover-dependent interactions
    - Add touch feedback (ripples, highlights)
    - Consider thumb zones (easier to reach bottom than top)
    
    ### Content Adaptation
    
    - Use `display: none` sparingly (still downloads)
    - Progressive enhancement (core content first, enhancements on larger screens)
    - Lazy loading for off-screen content
    - Responsive images (`srcset`, `picture` element)
    
    ### Navigation Adaptation
    
    - Transform complex nav to hamburger/drawer on mobile
    - Bottom nav bar for mobile apps
    - Persistent side navigation on desktop
    - Breadcrumbs on smaller screens for context
    
    Test on real devices. DevTools emulation is a useful first pass but misses real touch, performance, and browser chrome.
    
    **Avoid**:
    - Hide core functionality on mobile (if it matters, make it work)
    - Assume desktop = powerful device (consider accessibility, older machines)
    - Use different information architecture across contexts (confusing)
    - Break user expectations for platform (mobile users expect mobile patterns)
    - Forget landscape orientation on mobile/tablet
    - Use generic breakpoints blindly (use content-driven breakpoints)
    - Ignore touch on desktop (many desktop devices have touch)
    
    ## Verify Adaptations
    
    Test thoroughly across contexts:
    
    - **Real devices**: Test on actual phones, tablets, desktops
    - **Different orientations**: Portrait and landscape
    - **Different browsers**: Safari, Chrome, Firefox, Edge
    - **Different OS**: iOS, Android, Windows, macOS
    - **Different input methods**: Touch, mouse, keyboard
    - **Edge cases**: Very small screens (320px), very large screens (4K)
    - **Slow connections**: Test on throttled network
    
    **Custom controls** (sliders, drag surfaces, scrollable control strips): a before/after slider can pass every width check above and still refuse to drag on iOS, so exercise each one in scope in the same batched round as the checks above:
    
    - **Primary gesture**: Tap it and confirm it responds as designed, then drag it with the target input method; the drag must complete, not just start
    - **Scroll across it**: A swipe along the page's scroll axis across the control scrolls the page or container without activating it; a drag that starts on the control along its axis moves the control, not the page. Neither failure throws an error, so try both
    - **Evidence**: Say what produced the evidence: an emulated viewport, synthesized touch input through a browser tool, which engine ran it (Chromium is not Safari), or a physical device. Screenshots and resized viewports verify layout, never a gesture. Name what stayed untested and move on; unreachable hardware is a reported gap, not a blocker
    
    When the adaptation feels native to each context, hand off to `$impeccable polish` for the final pass.
    
    ---
    
    ## Reference Material
    
    The sections below were previously `responsive-design.md` and live inline now so the adapt flow has its deep responsive reference in one place.
    
    ### Responsive Design
    
    #### Mobile-First: Write It Right
    
    Start with base styles for mobile, use `min-width` queries to layer complexity. Desktop-first (`max-width`) means mobile loads unnecessary styles first.
    
    #### Breakpoints: Content-Driven
    
    Don't chase device sizes; let content tell you where to break. Start narrow, stretch until design breaks, add breakpoint there. Three breakpoints usually suffice (640, 768, 1024px). Use `clamp()` for fluid values without breakpoints.
    
    #### Detect Input Method, Not Just Screen Size
    
    **Screen size doesn't tell you input method.** A laptop with touchscreen, a tablet with keyboard. Use pointer and hover queries:
    
    ```css
    /* Fine pointer (mouse, trackpad) */
    @media (pointer: fine) {
      .button { padding: 8px 16px; }
    }
    
    /* Coarse pointer (touch, stylus) */
    @media (pointer: coarse) {
      .button { padding: 12px 20px; }  /* Larger touch target */
    }
    
    /* Device supports hover */
    @media (hover: hover) {
      .card:hover { transform: translateY(-2px); }
    }
    
    /* Device doesn't support hover (touch) */
    @media (hover: none) {
      .card { /* No hover state - use active instead */ }
    }
    ```
    
    **Critical**: Don't rely on hover for functionality. Touch users can't hover.
    
    #### Safe Areas: Handle the Notch
    
    Modern phones have notches, rounded corners, and home indicators. Use `env()`:
    
    ```css
    body {
      padding-top: env(safe-area-inset-top);
      padding-bottom: env(safe-area-inset-bottom);
      padding-left: env(safe-area-inset-left);
      padding-right: env(safe-area-inset-right);
    }
    
    /* With fallback */
    .footer {
      padding-bottom: max(1rem, env(safe-area-inset-bottom));
    }
    ```
    
    **Enable viewport-fit** in your meta tag:
    ```html
    <meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1, viewport-fit=cover">
    ```
    
    #### Responsive Images: Get It Right
    
    ##### srcset with Width Descriptors
    
    ```html
    <img
      src="hero-800.jpg"
      srcset="
        hero-400.jpg 400w,
        hero-800.jpg 800w,
        hero-1200.jpg 1200w
      "
      sizes="(max-width: 768px) 100vw, 50vw"
      alt="Hero image"
    >
    ```
    
    **How it works**:
    - `srcset` lists available images with their actual widths (`w` descriptors)
    - `sizes` tells the browser how wide the image will display
    - Browser picks the best file based on viewport width AND device pixel ratio
    
    ##### Picture Element for Art Direction
    
    When you need different crops/compositions (not just resolutions):
    
    ```html
    <picture>
      <source media="(min-width: 768px)" srcset="wide.jpg">
      <source media="(max-width: 767px)" srcset="tall.jpg">
      <img src="fallback.jpg" alt="...">
    </picture>
    ```
    
    #### Layout Adaptation Patterns
    
    **Navigation**: Three stages: hamburger + drawer on mobile, horizontal compact on tablet, full with labels on desktop. **Tables**: Transform to cards on mobile using `display: block` and `data-label` attributes. **Progressive disclosure**: Use `<details>/<summary>` for content that can collapse on mobile.
    
    #### Testing: Don't Trust DevTools Alone
    
    DevTools device emulation is useful for layout but misses:
    
    - Actual touch interactions
    - Real CPU/memory constraints
    - Network latency patterns
    - Font rendering differences
    - Browser chrome/keyboard appearances
    
    **Test on at least**: One real iPhone, one real Android, a tablet if relevant. Cheap Android phones reveal performance issues you'll never see on simulators.
    
    ---
    
    **Avoid**: Desktop-first design. Device detection instead of feature detection. Separate mobile/desktop codebases. Ignoring tablet and landscape. Assuming all mobile devices are powerful.
    # Craft floor
    
    Load this after the direction is settled, and build without announcing the checklist. A pinned brief or the committed visual world overrides anything here except a line marked as a ban; your own habit does not. When the design hook is active it already enforces the mechanical checks below as you edit: act on its findings instead of re-auditing each rule.
    
    ## Verify
    
    Each of these is a check on the built result, not an intention. Run them together in each batched look at the render, not as separate screenshot trips; the checks share one render.
    
    - **Contrast:** body and placeholder text ≥4.5:1, large text ≥3:1. On colored surfaces tint secondary text from that hue or the foreground; never gray.
    - **Depth:** shadows carry an offset and a soft blur. A zero-offset colored halo is decoration.
    - **Spacing:** tight groups, generous separation, more space above a heading than below it. Read the computed values.
    - **Type:** body measure 65–75ch, display max 6rem, tracking floor -0.04em, balanced headings, obvious scale and weight steps. Run the real copy at every breakpoint and fix what overflows.
    - **Motion:** one authored moment, not scattered effects and not one identical entrance on every section. Exponential ease-out from an already-visible default. Reach past transform and opacity: blur, backdrop-filter, clip-path, mask, and shadow belong to the palette when they stay smooth.
    - **States:** hover, disabled, loading, error, empty. Plus real content, working controls, responsive composition, keyboard focus.
    - **Browser surfaces:** the parts you did not draw still carry the design. Text selection, the caret, custom scrollbars, focus rings, underline offset, and the numerals in tabular data all ship with browser defaults that belong to no design system. Theme them from the palette. This is the cheapest signal that a page was built rather than assembled, and the one models skip most reliably.
    - **Copy:** the product's own language. Controls name their action; errors name the problem and the recovery.
    - **Coverage:** every brief requirement present and findable within seconds.
    
    ## Refuse
    
    These are the category's defaults, not bans: the brief's own words can earn any of them. Reaching for one when the axis is free means you were not deciding; recognizing that means rewriting the element, not softening it.
    
    Page scaffolds:
    
    - Same-size cards of icon plus heading plus text as the page structure. Cards are the lazy container; nested cards are always wrong.
    - The hero-metric template: big number, small label, supporting stats, accent.
    - A kicker or eyebrow above a heading. This one is a ban, not a default: no brief earns it back. The heading carries its own weight; delete the label and let the heading speak.
    - Section numbers (01 / 02 / 03) unless the sequence itself carries information the reader needs.
    - A modal for a task that needs neither interruption nor protected focus.
    
    Surface habits:
    
    - Gradient text. Emphasis comes from weight or size.
    - Glass and blur as decoration rather than as a specific effect.
    - A colored `border-left` or `border-right` above 1px on cards, list items, callouts, or alerts.
    - Hard offset shadows (`box-shadow: 4px 4px 0`) outside a world that is actually neobrutalist. The zero-blur block shadow is a costume, not a depth system; a world that did not choose it never earns it as a default.
    - Sparklines, progress rings, and soft-shadowed rounded rectangles standing in for content.
    - Monospace as a costume for "technical" rather than for code, data, or measurement.
    - A system display face (Impact, Arial Black, the platform sans) as the display voice of an own-world page. Source and self-host a face whose character matches the approved lettering; the closest installed font is a failure, not a fallback.
    - Unicode glyphs or emoji standing in for an icon system. Icons are drawn, from a real library or authored SVG, in one consistent stroke and weight.
    - Geometric masks standing in for organic contours. A circle, polygon, or radial-gradient cutout approximating a photographic subject's edge is the cheap version of the effect and reads worse than omitting it. Derive an alpha matte from the actual image, or produce a cut-out asset.
    - Light or dark picked by category. Pick it from the use scene: who, where, under what ambient light.
    
    - Tracking stops at -0.04em. -0.02 to -0.03em usually reads better.
    - Declare elevation once, border or shadow. A 1px border under a wide soft shadow is the ghost card. Card radii stay at 12–16px; pills are for small controls.
    - Real illustration or none. Sketch-style SVG scenes, `loose-sketch` / `doodle` class names, and `feTurbulence` grain read as amateur. This bans SVG imitating pictures, never SVG doing geometry: crisp vector shapes, diagrams, animated linework, and shader-driven effects remain first-class media. A shaded, perspectived, or figure-bearing illustration is a picture even in line-art style; geometry means shapes a session can specify exactly.
    - Backgrounds are surfaces, textured only from the subject's world. `repeating-linear-gradient` stripes and two-axis grid overlays need an actual canvas, map, blueprint, or measuring tool under them.
    - Claims and configuration come from supplied truth; label illustrative values honestly. Naming a concept and then ironizing it is not a claim.
    
    The floor holds the mechanics; it never picks the direction. With every check green, spend the page on the committed world, and when torn between refined and committed, commit.

    using Avalonia.Controls;
    using Avalonia.Interactivity;
    using Avalonia.Threading;
    using ResetExplode.Hardware;
    
    namespace ResetExplode;
    
    public partial class MainWindow
    {
        private CancellationTokenSource? _telemetryCancellation;
        private bool _telemetryStopped;
        private Task _telemetryTask = Task.CompletedTask;
        private TelemetrySnapshot? _snapshot;
        private CpuTemperatureReading? _thermalReading;
    
        private void ShowSystem(object? sender, RoutedEventArgs e) => SelectSystemPage(true);
        private void ShowBench(object? sender, RoutedEventArgs e) => SelectSystemPage(false);
    
        private void SelectSystemPage(bool system)
        {
            SystemPage.IsVisible = system;
            BenchPage.IsVisible = !system;
            SystemNav.Classes.Set("primary", system);
            SystemNav.Classes.Set("outline", !system);
            BenchNav.Classes.Set("primary", !system);
            BenchNav.Classes.Set("outline", system);
        }
    
        private void StartTelemetry()
        {
            if (_telemetryCancellation != null || _telemetryStopped) return;
            var cancellation = new CancellationTokenSource();
            _telemetryCancellation = cancellation;
            // Um único loop: nunca sobrepor consultas, mesmo quando um driver responde lentamente.
            _telemetryTask = Task.Run(async () =>
            {
                var token = cancellation.Token;
                try
                {
                    var files = new LinuxFiles();
                    using var monitor = new LinuxSensorMonitor(files, new LinuxNvml());
                    var system = new LinuxSystemInfoCollector(files).Collect();
                    await Dispatcher.UIThread.InvokeAsync(() =>
                    {
                        if (!_telemetryStopped)
                        {
                            SystemEntries.ItemsSource = system.Entries;
                            ApplySystemOverview(system);
                            _cpuDescription = system.Entries.FirstOrDefault(e => e.Name == "Processador")?.Value ?? "Indisponível";
                        }
                    });
                    while (!token.IsCancellationRequested)
                    {
                        var snapshot = monitor.Read(token);
                        // Publicar antes de aguardar a UI; o watchdog não depende do dispatcher.
                        Volatile.Write(ref _thermalReading,
                            new CpuTemperatureReading(snapshot.CpuTemperature, snapshot.CollectionStartedTimestamp));
                        await Dispatcher.UIThread.InvokeAsync(() =>
                        {
                            if (!_telemetryStopped) ApplySnapshot(snapshot);
                        });
                        await Task.Delay(TimeSpan.FromSeconds(1), token);
                    }
                }
                catch (OperationCanceledException) when (token.IsCancellationRequested) { }
                catch (Exception ex)
                {
                    Volatile.Write(ref _thermalReading, null);
                    if (!token.IsCancellationRequested)
                        await Dispatcher.UIThread.InvokeAsync(() =>
                        {
                            if (!_telemetryStopped)
                            {
                                _snapshot = null;
                                OverviewGpuText.Text = "Telemetria indisponível";
                                if (OverviewEntries.ItemsSource == null) OverviewMachineText.Text = "Resumo do sistema indisponível";
                                SensorEntries.ItemsSource = null;
                                SensorCountText.Text = "Leituras indisponíveis.";
                                TelemetryNoticesText.Text = "";
                                CpuSummaryText.Text = SidebarCpuText.Text = "CPU · indisponível";
                                RamSummaryText.Text = SidebarRamText.Text = "RAM · indisponível";
                                TelemetryStatusText.Text = $"Coleta interrompida: {ex.Message}";
                            }
                        });
                }
                finally { Volatile.Write(ref _thermalReading, null); cancellation.Dispose(); }
            });
        }
    
        private void StopTelemetry()
        {
            _telemetryStopped = true;
            Volatile.Write(ref _thermalReading, null);
            // O loop pode já ter terminado por falha; nesse caso seu CTS já foi descartado.
            try { _telemetryCancellation?.Cancel(); }
            catch (ObjectDisposedException) { }
        }
    
        private void ApplySnapshot(TelemetrySnapshot snapshot)
        {
            _snapshot = snapshot;
            ApplyGpuOverview(snapshot);
            string load = snapshot.CpuLoadPercent is double value ? $"{value:N1}%" : "indisponível";
            string temperature = snapshot.CpuTemperature is double temp ? $"{temp:N1} °C" : "temperatura indisponível";
            CpuSummaryText.Text = $"CPU · {load} · {temperature}";
            SidebarCpuText.Text = $"CPU · {load}\n{temperature}";
            if (snapshot.Memory.UsedBytes is long used && snapshot.Memory.TotalBytes is long total)
            {
                string ram = $"{used / 1073741824.0:N1} / {total / 1073741824.0:N1} GiB";
                RamSummaryText.Text = $"RAM · {ram} · disponível {snapshot.Memory.AvailableBytes!.Value / 1073741824.0:N1} GiB";
                SidebarRamText.Text = $"RAM · {ram}";
            }
            else RamSummaryText.Text = SidebarRamText.Text = "RAM · leitura indisponível";
            TelemetryStatusText.Text = $"Atualizado às {snapshot.Timestamp:HH:mm:ss} · leituras sequenciais a cada ~1 s";
            TelemetryNoticesText.Text = string.Join(Environment.NewLine, snapshot.Notices);
            ApplySensorFilter();
        }
    
        private void FilterSensors(object? sender, TextChangedEventArgs e)
        {
            if (_snapshot != null) ApplySensorFilter();
        }
    
        private void ApplySensorFilter()
        {
            if (_snapshot == null) return;
            string search = SensorSearch.Text?.Trim() ?? "";
            var rows = _snapshot.Sensors.Where(s => search.Length == 0 ||
                s.Hardware.Contains(search, StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase) ||
                s.Label.Contains(search, StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase)).ToArray();
            SensorEntries.ItemsSource = rows;
            SensorCountText.Text = $"{rows.Length} de {_snapshot.Sensors.Count} leituras · {rows.Count(s => s.Value != null)} disponíveis";
        }
    }

        public MainWindow()
        {
            InitializeComponent();
            InitializePersistence();
            MachineText.Text = $"{Environment.MachineName} · {Environment.ProcessorCount} threads lógicas · {RuntimeInformation.OSDescription}";
            _bitmap = new WriteableBitmap(new PixelSize(BenchRenderer.Width, BenchRenderer.Height),
                new Vector(96, 96), PixelFormat.Bgra8888, AlphaFormat.Opaque);
            RenderImage.Source = _bitmap;
            _renderer = new BenchRenderer();
            Array.Fill(_renderer.Pixels, unchecked((int)0xFF000000));
            RefreshBitmap();
            _renderer = null;
            _timer = new DispatcherTimer { Interval = TimeSpan.FromMilliseconds(200) };
            _timer.Tick += (_, _) => RefreshProgress();
            _thermalStatusTimer = new DispatcherTimer { Interval = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(1) };
            _thermalStatusTimer.Tick += (_, _) => UpdateThermalStatus();
            _thermalStatusTimer.Start();
            UpdateThermalStatus();
            Closing += OnClosing;
            Opened += async (_, _) => { StartTelemetry(); StartPowerMonitoring(); await Task.WhenAll(StartStorageRefresh(), StartSerialRefresh(), StartNetworkRefresh(), StartGpuDiscovery()); };
            Closed += (_, _) => { StopTelemetry(); _storageCancellation.Dispose(); _serialCancellation.Dispose(); _networkCancellation.Dispose(); _powerCancellation.Dispose(); _stabilityCancellation.Dispose(); _timer.Stop(); _thermalStatusTimer.Stop(); RenderImage.Source = null; _bitmap.Dispose(); };
        }

    > **Additional context needed**: target platforms/devices and usage contexts.
    
    Adapt an existing **native** design (`ios` / `android` / `adaptive`) to a different context: another device class, orientation, platform, or origin. The trap is treating adaptation as scaling. The job is rethinking the experience for the new context, inside the platform conventions of [ios.md](ios.md) / [android.md](android.md); read the target platform's reference before planning if Setup hasn't already.
    
    ## Assess Adaptation Challenge
    
    1. **Source context**: what was it designed for, and what assumptions did it make? (Phone-only? Portrait-only? One platform's idioms? A website?)
    2. **Target context**: which device class (phone, tablet, foldable), orientation, platform, and usage posture (one-handed on the go vs two-handed at rest)?
    3. **What breaks**: navigation that doesn't fit the target, layouts that stretch instead of restructure, gestures or controls that don't exist there?
    
    ## Adaptation Strategies
    
    ### Phone → Tablet (iPad / large screens)
    
    - **Restructure, don't stretch.** A scaled-up phone UI on a tablet is the failure mode. Use size classes (iOS) / window size classes (Android) to switch structure.
    - **Navigation changes shape**: tab bar stays or becomes a sidebar on iPad; Android navigation bar becomes a rail or drawer on expanded width.
    - **Use the width**: split view / master-detail (list + detail side by side), multi-column grids, popovers where phones used sheets.
    - **Multitasking is a size, not an edge case**: iPad Split View and Android multi-window can hand you a phone-width window on a tablet; size-class-driven layout handles both for free.
    
    ### Orientation & foldables
    
    - Landscape restructures (side-by-side panes, repositioned controls); never clip or letterbox. Lock orientation only when the task truly demands it.
    - Foldables (Android): react to posture and hinge via window size classes; test folded, unfolded, and tabletop.
    
    ### Platform → platform (iOS ↔ Android)
    
    Translate idioms; never transplant them:
    
    | iOS | Android |
    |---|---|
    | Tab bar | Navigation bar / rail / drawer |
    | Edge-swipe back, back chevron | Predictive Back gesture / button |
    | Switch, segmented control, system pickers | Material switch, chips, Material pickers |
    | Action sheet | Bottom sheet / Material dialog |
    | SF Symbols, SF Pro, Dynamic Type | Material Symbols, Roboto, sp scaling |
    | Semantic system colors, materials | Material color roles, tonal elevation |
    | System push/sheet transitions | Container transform, shared-axis, fade-through |
    
    Rebuild navigation and controls in the target's vocabulary; carry over the brand's expressive layer (palette intent, type accent, motion personality) through the target's theming system.
    
    ### Web → native (porting a website or web app)
    
    Reconform, don't reflow. Replace web navigation with the platform's model, HTML-shaped controls with platform controls, hover affordances with touch-first ones, and px-based type with Dynamic Type / sp. Then treat the result to the full platform reference; the slop test there is the acceptance bar.
    
    ## Implement & Verify
    
    - Drive structure from **size classes / window size classes**, never from device-model checks.
    - Respect safe areas and window insets in every new configuration (notch, hinge, status bar, keyboard).
    - Test on simulators for breadth, then real hardware for truth: at least one phone and one tablet per shipped platform, both orientations, split-screen where supported.
    
    When the adaptation feels native to each context, hand off to `$impeccable polish` for the final pass.
    
    **Avoid**:
    - Ship a stretched phone layout on a tablet
    - Port one platform's controls or navigation onto the other
    - Hide core functionality on smaller devices (if it matters, make it work)
    - Lock orientation to dodge a layout bug
    - Trust simulators alone (posture, gestures, and performance need hardware)
